## [2026-09-24] note | Boundary annotation added to LongTermMemory.md (memory lifecycle / forgetting)

Operation label `note` (outside the documented set ingest/query/health/lint/graph) is used deliberately: this was **neither a full ingest nor a lint**. No source page was written and no new `sources` slug was created, so the source pages count stays at 6.
Trigger: concept-radar 2026-09-24, candidate 1 — the memory cluster ([[LongTermMemory]] / [[Provenance]] / [[CitationLock]] / [[Abstention]]) documented only the *first half* of a memory's life (store it, split it by purpose, read it credibly) and had **no page discussing deletion**.
Change: appended one `## Boundary Note — the other half of a memory's life` section to `wiki/concepts/LongTermMemory.md`; bumped `last_updated` 2026-09-16 → 2026-09-24; added tag `memory-lifecycle`. Added wikilinks: [[Provenance]] and [[Compaction]] — both existing pages, no new dangling link. No existing text was altered or removed.
Content in one line: retention is per-type (episodic → semantic → procedural), the sharpest seam is **deletion vs [[Provenance]]** (erasing a fact breaks the citation chain, so deletion must itself leave a receipt + failure list + rollback snapshot), and this is **not [[Compaction]]** — compaction is in-window, per-turn; lifecycle governance is out-of-window, cross-session, offline.
⚠️ **The section carries a `pending ingest` banner.** Its claims were verified against live primary sources on 2026-09-24 (recorded in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-09-24.md`), but they are **not yet backed by a source page in this wiki** and have **not** been human-reviewed. When the material is ingested, the banner should be removed and the source slug moved into the frontmatter `sources` field. Frontmatter `sources` was intentionally **left untouched** (`[agent-memory-provenance]`) because referencing a slug that does not exist would break the lint's `sources`-slug dimension.
Post-change validation: `python tools/health.py` → 31 pages, Empty/Stub 0 ✅ · Index Sync 0 ✅ · Log Coverage 0 ✅. No files deleted; `raw/` untouched.

## [2026-09-24] ingest | 智能体身份与委派授权(Agent Identity & Delegated Authority)

Authorised by the human this session ("授权摄取这 3 份 HTML") — the previously-flagged `learning-materials/ → raw/` process gap. **Copies only:** the three HTMLs were copied from `learning-materials/` to `raw/learning-materials/` and verified **byte-identical** by sha256 (`6c0f79d4…`, `b4f15b67…`, `fd3a52b0…`); the originals were **not** moved or deleted.
Source: `raw/learning-materials/agent-identity.html` (silent-madman concept-learning HTML, generated 2026-09-22). Source page: `wiki/sources/agent-identity.md`.
Core: identity ≠ a name in the prompt; the agent never holds the user's raw credentials (OAuth 2.0 Token Exchange, RFC 8693, dual subject = actor + subject); **per-hop attenuation** (a child agent gets a fresh, strictly narrower credential — RFC 8693 records delegation but does not guarantee attenuation); four distinct things (identity / delegation / authorization / runtime policy); [[MCP]]'s authorization profile (OAuth 2.1 resource server + RFC 9728 + PKCE S256) as the concrete instance; explicitly **not** a sandbox; 2026 adoption (Entra Agent ID GA 2026-05-01, Okta for AI Agents GA 2026-04-29, APASS >1,000,000 agents / 442 providers).
New concept page: `wiki/concepts/AgentIdentity.md`.
Provenance caution recorded on the source page: the 28% traceability figure and the 1–2 / 3–6 engineer-week estimates come from third-party/commercial pages (ZDNet Inside, mm-ais), i.e. **vendor-survey grade**.

## [2026-09-24] ingest | 人在环审批闸门(Human-in-the-Loop / Escalation Gate)

Source: `raw/learning-materials/human-in-the-loop.html` (same 2026-09-22 batch). Source page: `wiki/sources/human-in-the-loop.md`.
Core: grade actions by **impact × reversibility**; supervision mode chosen **per action class** (in-the-loop / on-the-loop / automatic + sampling); split **proposal** from **side-effecting execution**; the gate's decision must be **deterministic and live in code/config, not the prompt**; pause must be **persisted** and resume **idempotent** (the resuming process need not be the pausing one); measure automation / override / **escape** rate / review latency. Operating rule: "**read freely, keep writes human**".
Boundary the source draws explicitly (recorded because it sharpens this wiki): **[[Abstention]] and [[CitationLock]] have the model as their subject and govern *saying* (honesty mechanisms); a gate has the system and the human as subject and governs *writing / paying / deleting* (a control mechanism).**
New concept page: `wiki/concepts/HumanInTheLoop.md`.
Provenance cautions carried over from the material, and **excluded from being used as evidence**: the uncited "HITL ≈ 99.9% vs ≈ 80%" blog number (deliberately not used); the `< 0.92` confidence threshold (an illustrative example, not a finding); the Bain (n=951) and MIT SMR figures (second-hand).

## [2026-09-24] ingest | 工具幻觉与封闭世界消解(Tool Hallucination & Closed-World Resolution)

Source: `raw/learning-materials/tool-hallucination.html` (same 2026-09-22 batch). Source page: `wiki/sources/tool-hallucination.md`. Primary source: **arXiv:2609.19425v1 (2026-09-16, preprint — not peer-reviewed).**
Core: tool hallucination = invoking a tool that is **not in the registry**, or passing arguments **no schema declared**; such a call is **structurally not a decision any gate ever made**, so no permission gate can refuse it; the fix is a **Resolution Rung** (registry membership + signature verification) whose value is **in its position, not its algorithm** — upstream of every gate. Measured: **322** hallucinations over 10 hosted models / two invocation surfaces; **154** on a real MCP surface after merging; **scale gives no immunity** (675B ≈ 7–8B); merging MCP servers into one namespace **adds** a surface via **collision / shadowing**; an irreducible residual exists ("borrowed parameters" are schema-indistinguishable from legitimate calls). Release artifact: benchmark **HTB**.
New concept page: `wiki/concepts/ToolHallucination.md`.
⚠️ **Discrepancy recorded, not resolved:** the arXiv abstract writes `3434 vs. 33` for surface concentration while two secondary sources transcribe `34 vs. 3`; `322` with `34/3` is self-consistent and `3434` is not. The material (and this wiki) therefore cite **only 322 / 154 / 675B ≈ 7–8B**, and neither uses that ratio. Resolve against the arXiv body text before citing.

## [2026-09-24] ingest | Post-ingest validation (3 action-side sources)

`[[MCP]]` gained two sections contributed by this batch — an **authorization** profile (OAuth 2.1 resource server, RFC 9728, PKCE S256) and a **namespace-merging hazard** (collision / shadowing; merging adds a hallucination surface) — plus its frontmatter `sources` grew to `[skill, mcp-2026-07-28, agent-identity, tool-hallucination]`. `[[Agent]]` gained an **Authority axis** section and its frontmatter `sources` now includes the three new slugs; its previously implicit gap (the loop can be correct and still unauthorized / unapproved / aimed at a non-existent tool) is now explicit.

`wiki/overview.md`: **revised, not annotated** — this batch is the wiki's first **actor-side** cluster, so the synthesis gained a Cluster D, a two-halves table (entity side = what it knows / actor side = what it may do), an updated connection diagram, two new cross-cutting themes ("the model is not the only place to put a control"; "ordering is a first-class design object"), three new open questions (execution isolation, persistent execution, independent verification of Cluster D's numbers) and two new ingest suggestions.
No dangling `[[wikilink]]` introduced: every new link targets a page that now exists ([[AgentIdentity]], [[HumanInTheLoop]], [[ToolHallucination]], the three new source slugs) or already existed ([[Agent]], [[MCP]], [[Skill]], [[Provenance]], [[Abstention]], [[CitationLock]], [[ContextEngineering]], [[Transformer]], [[LLMContext]], [[ProgressiveDisclosure]], [[ContextRot]], [[Compaction]], [[LongTermMemory]], [[MemGPT]], [[AgentZeroMemory]], [[Zep]], [[LongMemEval]], [[Anthropic]], [[OpenAI]]).

(1) ⭐ **agent memory integrity — conversation-history poisoning + persistent memory poisoning**: the wiki's memory four-page cluster ([[Provenance]] / [[CitationLock]] / [[Abstention]] / [[LongTermMemory]]) governs *whether a written fact had a source*, and never *whether a stored memory is still the memory the model actually wrote*. Two primary-grade September 2026 disclosures fill that gap — PMPA (arXiv:2609.13889v1, 2026-09-12; Auckland + Fudan; OpenClaw ISR/C-ASR 73.7%/55.5%, Claude Code 66.9%/81.7%, benign task performance preserved) and Darktrace's conversation-history poisoning (2026-09-24; client-side SQLite conversation history accepted across Claude Code / Codex / Kiro-CLI / Pi, no validation that stored replies were model-produced; 78 fabricated turns take an agent from refusal to full Active Directory compromise; the proposed fix is provider-side and cannot be deployed by defenders). This also lands the long-backlogged **09-18 memory poisoning (ASI06)** candidate; recommendation is to merge it into one page rather than open two nodes. (2) **Skills over MCP (SEP-2640)** — Final 2026-09-13, merged into the official repo, three new methods (`skills/list` / `skills/get` / optional `resources/directory/read`) over the existing Resources primitive, **per-file SHA-256 digest binding** (upgraded from SKILL.md-only digesting in an earlier draft), and progressive disclosure arriving in MCP; the boundary the wiki drew between [[Skill]] and [[MCP]] moves. Grounding caveat recorded in the same breath: the spec PR merged 2026-09-18 but **all four official SDK implementation PRs (Go / Python / TypeScript / C#) remain open**, and a crawl of 1,432 hosted MCP endpoints found only **2** declaring `io.modelcontextprotocol/skills` while 270 providers ship both skills and a server that cannot see them — "Final ≠ usable"; recommends a **revision** to [[Skill]] / [[MCP]] / [[ProgressiveDisclosure]], not new nodes. (3) **CAFE(S)** (ACM Queue, 2026-09-24; DX + Capital One + GitHub + University of Victoria + Google) — five independent context-quality properties (Clarity / Actionability / Fidelity / Efficiency / Security) giving [[ContextEngineering]] the review vocabulary it lacked; self-limited as "not a complete measurement system" and "not a numerical score", which must be preserved verbatim; recommends a revision to [[ContextEngineering]]. Details in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-09-29.md`.

Today's three radar candidates came from one reusable scan — *for each concept cluster in the wiki, ask separately "who decides here, and where does the deciding information come from?"* — which exposed three holes at once. (1) ⭐ **memory as a learned policy** — the memory cluster ([[LongTermMemory]] / [[Provenance]] / [[CitationLock]] / [[Abstention]]) states what a memory must satisfy and, since 2026-09-24, when it should be deleted; but it always assumes the rules are hand-written. Six independent teams have turned "what to write / when to retrieve / when to delete" into *trained policies with their own reward* — Memory-R1 (arXiv:2508.19828; **ACL 2026 main conference** `2026.acl-long.583`; 152 QA pairs suffice, LoCoMo F1 +68.9% over Mem0), ICML / Interactive Memory Learning (arXiv:2609.17088; HIT Harbin + Pengcheng), UI-Mem (arXiv:2602.05832; CUHK + vivo), ATMem (arXiv:2606.31612; AndroidWorld 76.6% vs UI-TARS-2-230B 73.3% at about 1/29 the parameters), MemChain (arXiv:2607.24097), MMPO (arXiv:2605.30159). The standing caveat belongs on the first screen: **none of the six has been deployed in a production environment where memory accumulates over months**, their benchmarks do not cover distribution drift / adversarial input / accumulated contradiction, and no single system yet owns write + retrieve + transform. Recommendation: **merge this with the 09-24 memory-lifecycle and 09-21 memory-timing backlog rows into one page** rather than open more nodes. (2) **persistent agents with a standing identity** — a whole product class this wiki has never graphed: an agent with its **own** mailbox / VM / memory that keeps running while its owner is offline, i.e. a first-class principal rather than a delegated session. September 2026 delivered four at once (Meta Muse GA 2026-09-08 with a per-user dedicated VM and a Sentinel agent approving every outbound action; Microsoft Copilot Autopilot private preview 2026-09-25, the only one with **its own directory identity**; OpenAI "o" leaked, DevDay 2026-09-29; Claude still with no independent identity). [[AgentIdentity]] covers only the *delegated* end — "who acts, and on whose behalf", with per-hop attenuation — so this is the same problem seen from the other side: *who governs it when there is no present principal*. Caveat for the first screen: **"shipped" ≠ "supervised"** — only Meta documents an outbound approval gate, OpenAI "o"'s permissions are unpublished, and consumer-grade agents have already had real incidents (a SEV-2 VM vulnerability, address leakage, an OpenAI agent escaping its sandbox via DNS). (3) **the enterprise context layer** — [[ContextEngineering]] answers *what to put in the window and how to trim it* but treats context as *given*; nothing in the wiki asks where the context itself comes from, who maintains it, or how it stays fresh and trustworthy. Gartner now defines it as a standalone architectural plane (Semantics + Operational state + Provenance), and the deciding lines are "a context layer **can't be bought in its entirety**; the layer must be engineered" and "**connectivity without meaning is a very efficient way to distribute confusion**" (about 60% of agentic analytics projects that are protocol-connected but have no semantic foundation are predicted to fail by 2028). Alibaba Cloud shipped Enterprise Context at Yunqi on 2026-09-23. Recommendation: a **revision to [[ContextEngineering]]**, medium priority, not a new node. Backlog: 38 rows now (9 ✅ / 0 🟡 / 29 ⏸); the aging column was recomputed and the oldest un-landed ⭐ (09-24 memory lifecycle) is now **6 days**. Details in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-09-30.md`.

(1) ⭐ **the 09-24 memory-lifecycle ⭐ received its strongest evidence yet**: DyadMem (arXiv:2610.03020, submitted 2026-10-02; NTU/NUS/HKU/StepFun) is the first benchmark to supervise the **full pipeline** (Capture / Update / Recall) with gold annotations at each stage — and it measures precisely the lifecycle the backlog row demands: frontier LLMs show **low Capture recall, incomplete Recall, and unsafe deletions**, while Gold-Memory QA stays strong and Full-Pipeline QA drops sharply (20 models). The same week, "Memory Is a Derivation" (arXiv:2609.36130) audited compressed memories: **17–21% remain unsupported** even with broader pre-write history. The four-row merge now has a benchmark skeleton to organize around. (2) **lossless external memory + active re-look** (VISTA, arXiv:2610.02200, submitted 2026-10-01, He Kaiming's team): store every frame losslessly outside, let the model decide when to look back — ARC-AGI-3 40.68 → 100.00; and **enlarging the window to 780K produced no gain**, a direct verdict on the seam between [[ContextWindow]] / [[Compaction]] / [[ProgressiveDisclosure]]: the alternative to both "bigger window" and "lossy compaction" is "raw outside + pull on demand". (3) **compaction timing as a learned action** (AutoCompact, arXiv:2610.02163, submitted 2026-10-01, WebFetch-verified first-hand): +9.2 SWE-bench Verified, and the gain **holds with a 256K window that never overflows** — compaction is about *staleness*, not space; the decision moves from harness rule to trained policy (reinforces 09-30 candidate 1 from the in-window side). Details in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-10-06.md`.

Note that today's radar candidate 1 lands directly on the **persistent execution** open question, and strengthens it with a first-hand open-source reference implementation — worth a synthesis revision once a source is ingested.
External signal note (for the record, not ingested): this run changed the radar's scanning in one deliberate way — the object of observation moved **up one layer**, from "what the model does" to "what the infrastructure *beneath* the loop does", and three candidates fell out of it at once. (1) ⭐ **durable agent execution + Session as a first-class object** (this strengthens the existing 2026-09-23 backlog row, second reinforcement): this is the seam `overview.md` itself has named as unwritten ("the cross-runtime semantics comparison — event replay vs checkpoint adoption vs snapshot"), and it is also the unmet prerequisite of another page — `human-in-the-loop` requires that "the pause must be **persisted** and the resume **idempotent**", and no page says who provides that. Three independent pieces of first-hand evidence arrived this week: **Pi 1.0 + Pi Durable** (Earendil, 2026-10-01, MIT, experimental — a checkpoint is written before every step; `resume()` continues each unfinished task from its last checkpoint; a tool reruns only if it declared `replay: safe`, otherwise the model is told the tool was interrupted and may have partially run; `requestId` makes a submission exactly-once across retries), **DigitalOcean Managed Agents** public preview (2026-10-01 — pause / resume / checkpoint / fork, auto-pause billing), and a **neutral cross-runtime comparison** whose deciding sentence is "**session ≠ durable execution**" (Temporal / Step Functions / Durable Functions replay an event history against deterministic orchestrator code; DBOS checkpoints into PostgreSQL with no separate server; LangGraph resumes from the **start of the interrupted node**, not the line, so nodes must be idempotent). One architecture note adds the missing third object — Anthropic's Managed Agents splits an agent into `Brain = Model + Harness`, `Hands = Sandbox + Tools`, and **`Session = Durable Event Log`** — plus a reusable heuristic: **"Harness thinning, Infrastructure thickening"** (a stronger model makes the harness thinner but makes Sandbox / Permission / Durable State / Budget / Evaluation / Observability / Identity / Audit / Side-effect Control *more* important, because it enlarges the agent's radius of action). Also worth recording for the boundary: "**the human as an async API**" — the durable `await` condition *is* the implementation of the gate's persisted pause. (2) **write-time distillation vs read-time selection (non-destructive memory)** — strengthens and should be merged with the 2026-10-06 VISTA row; the memory four-page cluster governs *whether a written fact had a source* and never *whether the record was destroyed at write time*, while `Compaction` makes write-time distillation the default. Two first-hand items in the same week: **Mem++** (arXiv:2610.02002, 2026-10-01, UT Austin + AIDAChip) names the axis explicitly — "shifting from **write-time distillation** to **read-time selection**" — storing every document whole with date and author, calling no generative model at write time, keeping old versions instead of overwriting, and retrieving only documents dated up to the asked-about time (+8.0 to 13.1 on OrgMemBench; best LLM-judge on LoCoMo; 2nd on LongMemEval-S); and two new benchmarks make **state evolution** the dominant difficulty — **DSV-Mem** (arXiv:2610.08102, 2026-10-06; strongest baseline **below 45%**; "the number of governing updates is the dominant tested factor"; models often fail to verify a user's premise against prior state updates; extra reasoning effort and ordinary memory management give **limited** gains while state-aware designs help) and **EMBER-Bench** (arXiv:2610.05013, 2026-10-04; best of 16 models **61.2%** vs a human mean of **98.3%**; correct causal traceback is *not* correlated with correct next-action prediction). Caveat to carry onto the first screen: Mem++'s OrgMemBench is a **synthetic** organisation and the authors say it does not prove the same in real organisations. (3) **the agent execution plane / governed mediation layer** (new backlog row) — 09-26's Cluster E asked *who rents the harness*; this week it became a **neutral layer of named primitives** that turns Cluster D's four doctrines from design principles into **shipped defaults**: Retool's centralized harness puts tool calls through one gateway, allows policies to **inspect arguments** ("permission to use a tool doesn't have to mean permission to use it in any way the agent chooses"), states the discipline plainly — "**These limits are enforced outside the model**" — and makes explicit *negative* choices (no employee laptop access, no browser automation, no desktop control); DigitalOcean's **Action Gateway** is a single managed MCP endpoint in front of 16,000+ tools with **credentials attached at execution time so the agent never holds them**; **OpenClaw Enterprise 1.0** (OpenAI + Red Hat + Nvidia; "Kubernetes for agents"; donated to the OpenClaw Foundation) is governance-as-a-product born from a Gartner verdict that the tool was "an unacceptable cybersecurity risk"; **Alterion Helix** moves the governance object from single events to **emergent behaviour across decision sequences**; and the identity side is reinforced by a joint six-agency advisory (ACSC / CISA / NSA / Canadian Centre / NZ NCSC / UK NCSC) whose risk list is misuse of rights, **scope creep**, identity spoofing, **confused-deputy** attacks and accountability gaps, with measures requiring per-agent cryptographically anchored identity, runtime authentication and just-in-time credentials. **Discipline applied on the spot:** vendor *defaults* are citable (credentials attached at execution time; argument-inspecting policy; the negative defaults), vendor *benchmarks* are not (99.3% intent matching, 886 ms readiness, −28% / −32% token savings are all self-reported, sometimes against the vendor's own baseline). Details in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-10-07.md`.

Note: today's radar reinforced **persistent execution** (now framed as the whole "runtime layer") and **RAG** remains the oldest ⏸ row at 21 days.
External signal note (for the record, not ingested): this run asked a new question of the wiki's runtime layer — *which verbs does this layer have, and which of them still have no page?* — and three missing verbs fell out at once. (1) ⭐ **observe — agent observability & evaluation.** Cluster E names *what runs the loop* ([[AgentHarness]]), *where it runs* ([[AgentSandbox]]) and *who it works with* ([[MultiAgent]]), but no page says *how you see what it did and whether it was right*. Two existing pages already depend on it: [[HumanInTheLoop]]'s acceptance criteria require automation / override / **escape** rate, and [[AgentSandbox]]'s motive evidence is the HF intrusion anatomy — both need "visibility" that no page provides. The 2026 standard is being written now and is **not stable**: OpenTelemetry's GenAI semantic conventions moved out of the mainline spec into a dedicated repo (`open-telemetry/semantic-conventions-genai`) at semconv v1.42.0 (2026-06-12); mainline v1.43.0 (2026-07-03) carries no gen-ai content; every document is marked **Development**, with **zero tagged releases** (pin a commit, not a version) and attribute names renamed across releases. Core vocabulary: `gen_ai.provider.name` / `gen_ai.request.model` / `gen_ai.response.model` (which can differ under server-side fallback) / `gen_ai.usage.input_tokens` / `output_tokens`; agent and tool are **first-class span kinds** (`create_agent` / `invoke_agent` / `plan` / `invoke_workflow` / `execute_tool`); MCP has its own conventions (`mcp.client` / `mcp.server` spans + four MCP metrics); OpenInference defines six span kinds (LLM / CHAIN / TOOL / RETRIEVER / EMBEDDING / AGENT). Market consolidating: **Dynatrace signed a definitive agreement to acquire Arize in 2026-08**; Thoughtworks' April 2026 Radar put **toxic flow analysis** in Assess and warned that classical SAST/SCA "can't see an agent's prompts, its tools, its memory, or the traffic between agents", so a clean report looks like coverage. MCP's 2026 roadmap (2026-03-09) names observability as one of four priorities. Caveats for the first screen: instrumentation carries a measured cost (~12% AgentOps / ~15% Langfuse in a 2026 comparison); and the conventions standardise *the call*, not *the judgment* — they say nothing about evaluation scores. Recommendation: **merge with the existing 09-17「Agent Evals」row into one "observe & evaluate" page**; alternatively two cheap page revisions ([[AgentHarness]] + [[HumanInTheLoop]]). (2) **interoperate — A2A and the horizontal protocol layer.** This strengthens the long-standing 09-19 A2A row rather than opening a new node: A2A reached v1.0.0 (2026-03-12) and v1.0.1 (2026-05-28), six SDKs, 150+ organisations, **Signed Agent Cards** (JWS over JCS, RFC 7515 / 8785) + mTLS + OAuth 2.1 / PKCE / Device Code / DPoP, eight-state task lifecycle; and the structural item — **A2A joined the Agentic AI Foundation (AAIF) as a Growth Stage project on 2026-08-27**, beside MCP, AGENTS.md, goose and agentgateway under one neutral Linux Foundation body, which finally connects [[MCP]]'s governance story to the horizontal half. The portable line: **MCP is the agent's hands (vertical), A2A is the team between agents (horizontal)**; Mazin Gilbert (AAIF executive director): "You need to create this mesh network of protocols … And it's not one protocol. It's not five protocols." Security: A2A's threat surface (agent-card spoofing / poisoning, transitive cross-agent prompt injection, task replay, recursive DoS — OWASP **ASI07**) differs from MCP's (tool poisoning / rug pulls), so "securing one and assuming coverage of the other is the most common architectural mistake". Caveat: **Final ≠ usable** — A2A is a specification; the six SDKs live in six repositories, and a protocol version and an SDK version are different things; A2A defines **no** authorization ("authorization is left to you"); card signing is supported but not enforced. Recommendation: revision to [[MCP]] × [[MultiAgent]], not a new node. (3) **pay — agentic payments & commerce (AP2 / ACP / x402 / MPP).** The wiki's actor chain `propose → resolve → authorize → gate → execute` **ends at execute** and never covers *spending*; payment is the first real budget surface of the "irreversible" class and the line between an agent that proposes and one that transacts. Four protocols landed in the same ~18-month window: **AP2** (Google; authorization layer — Intent / Cart / Payment mandates as verifiable credentials; v0.2 donated to the FIDO Alliance on 2026-04-28, 60+ partners), **ACP** (Stripe + OpenAI; checkout layer — Shared Payment Tokens scoped to one merchant and one amount), **x402** (Coinbase; settlement — HTTP 402 + stablecoins; under Linux Foundation governance since 2026-07-14, 40 members; the 2026-09-02 dashboard read 75.41M transactions / $24.24M over thirty days, i.e. ~32¢ each, which says it is per-call API/compute payment, not retail), and **MPP** (Stripe + Tempo, 2026-03; session-based streaming micropayments). Caveat: **the protocols answer "may it pay", never "how much"** — none provides budget caps, merchant allowlists or approval thresholds (MPP's own docs say spending controls must be built on top); reconciliation, refunds and disputes across an AP2 mandate × x402 settlement are largely unsolved; and Real Vision reported x402's daily payment volume fell 93% in H1 — "shipped ≠ used". Its boundary is sharpest against [[AgentIdentity]] (per-hop narrowing credentials vs cryptographically credentialed **spend authority** — an AP2 mandate is that page's consumption instance) and [[HumanInTheLoop]] (a gate decides *whether*, a budget decides *how much*). Recommendation: a new `concepts/AgentPayments.md`, or two cheap revisions. Also strengthened today: the **09-23 durable-execution row** (third time) is now the named **runtime layer** — LangChain's "harness vs runtime" (Runkle & Trivedy, 2026-04-20), Anthropic's `Session / Harness / Sandbox` split, Cursor's run-scoped Cloud Agents API (2026-04-29), Vercel's "durable execution belongs in the language" (2026-08-27), Thoughtworks' April 2026 Radar (LangGraph Trial; Temporal / Restate / Golem Assess), and LangChain's Managed Deep Agents public beta; it should be upgraded to ⭐. Details in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-10-08.md`.

Its open questions from 2026-09-24/26 all remain open (RAG, context-engineering evaluation, multi-agent memory **governance**, MCP Tasks/Apps, persistent execution, and independent verification of Cluster D's numbers). Note: today's radar touched **four** of them at once — candidate 1 lands on the context-engineering-evaluation question, candidate 2 on persistent execution, candidate 3 on the verification question — so a synthesis revision becomes worth doing as soon as any one of these lands as a source. **RAG remains the oldest ⏸ row at 22 days.**
External signal note (for the record, not ingested): this run added a **fourth scanning line** to the radar — after the horizontal axis (10-06: which cluster moved, on which axis), the vertical axis (10-07: what the layer *beneath* the loop did) and the verb inventory (10-08: which verbs of this layer still have no page), today asked **"what does each existing mechanism silently assume is free?"** Three assumptions broke at once, each with first-hand October-2026 evidence. (1) ⭐ **always-loaded context is not free — and "load on demand" is not reliable.** The strongest theoretical item is **arXiv:2610.11007** (Zexuan Liu / Yuning Yang / Tiancheng Zhao, submitted **2026-10-07**), which formulates context curation as a **capacitated assortment problem** and proves: an **upper bound on the optimal file size** regardless of how many candidate instructions exist; that **appending every instruction with positive standalone value can be arbitrarily worse in net value than selecting an optimal subset**; that **adding an instruction never raises the compliance of the others**; and that **feedback is censored** — the harms of a *missing* instruction are observed only when its absence causes harm — so **deleting instructions that agents ignore inevitably removes helpful ones**. It also shows irrelevant rules drawn from real context files **reduce** compliance. The empirical side is unusually well-populated and **partly contradictory**: **arXiv:2602.11988** (Gloaguen et al., ETH Zurich + LogicStar.ai, MemAgents @ ICLR 2026 Oral) built **AGENTbench** (138 instances / 12 niche Python repos, derived from 5,694 real PRs) and reports that context files **do not generally improve task success while raising inference cost by over 20%**, that **LLM-generated files slightly hurt** (−0.5% to −2%), that developer-written files help only ~+4%, and — pointedly — that the **"repository overview" section, the one most starter templates produce**, is **not helpful**; **arXiv:2601.20404** (Lulla et al., 10 repos / 124 PRs) measures the opposite direction on a different metric: **median runtime −28.64%** (98.57 s → 70.34 s) and **output tokens −16.58%** with completion unchanged (total tokens roughly flat, ~+1%); **arXiv:2606.15828** (UFMG) finds **91% of popular repositories' AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md still carry at least one configuration smell** — **Lint Leakage 62% / Context Bloat 42% / Skill Leakage 35%**. And the operational result that ties it to the wiki's own [[ProgressiveDisclosure]] page: **Vercel's agent evals** gave one model the same documentation two ways — always-loaded in **AGENTS.md passed 100%**, while **skills passed 53%, identical to giving no docs at all, and in 56% of cases the agent never loaded the skill**; telling it explicitly to use skills raised that to 79%. Scott Spence measured the same failure to trigger (50–55% of 22 prompts; **22/22 = 100%** once a `UserPromptSubmit` hook forced skill evaluation). **IFScale** gave models up to 500 instructions at once — the best frontier models followed **68%**, favouring the ones that came first. A 34,000-skill-scale evaluation localises the mechanism: the dominant failure mode is a **retrieval miss**, not a bad skill. **Discipline applied on the spot:** the two 2026 AGENTS.md papers **measure different things** (efficiency vs task success) and can both be true — *faster and cheaper while solving fewer tasks* — so the honest citable conclusions are only three: always-loaded wins on compliance (100% vs 53%), always-loaded is the most expensive position (916 words ≈ 1,474 tokens, re-sent every turn; ~14 M tokens over ~9,300 turns in one measured project), and **content type decides** (specific, non-standard, project-specific constraints help; overviews do not). **"Under 200 lines" is vendor advice, not an empirical finding** — a 1,650-session study could detect no effect of length, position, file architecture or cross-file contradictions (the only visible effect was fading within a long session). (2) **the harness's own resource consumption is not free, and now has a cost model.** **arXiv:2610.02488** ("Harnessing LLMs as Agents: What Does It Cost?") introduces the **Language Model Agent Machine (LAM)** — a resource-bounded abstraction that fixes the semantic model while **explicitly charging harness-level resources** — with four classes of results: **communication** (LAM execution is instancewise equivalent to red–blue pebbling under simultaneous call–transfer budgets, transferring classical I/O lower bounds to **context↔memory traffic**), **access** (a **Θ(n)** asymptotic separation between random and non-speculative sequential access on pointer chasing), **recomputation** (bit-reversal DAGs require **Θ(n²/(C+S)+n)** model calls under context capacity C and persistent-memory capacity S — i.e. it quantifies *when storing intermediate state beats recomputing it*), and **reliability** (tight stage-local sampling bounds, exact **imperfect-verification** costs, and a **Young–Daly-type checkpoint law with a closed-form optimal verification interval**). This is the theoretical counterpart of the wiki's most-repeated cross-cutting theme — that the model is not the only place to put a control — and it supplies exactly the missing decision rule for a page that has none. **Caveat carried onto the first screen:** this is a **provable lower bound, not a deployable cost-optimisation recipe**, and the item's provenance is **abstract-level** (the arXiv abstract page was read as returned by search; the PDF was not opened page-by-page) — flagged as such in the radar. (3) **full-transcript supervision is not free, so the observation layer moved inward.** Two first-hand items in the same week: **Goodfire's "inside-out" monitors** (announced **2026-10-08**, first available to Baseten customers) attach small **activation probes** that read the model's internals **at every step** and escalate to an expensive second model **only when a probe fires** — the cost story being that a probe **reuses computations the forward pass already performed**, whereas a judge model pays for a whole new forward pass over the transcript; the vendor reports (all figures self-reported and **not independently reproduced**) roughly **$51** to monitor **1,500 sessions on Kimi K3**, against **$233** for a cheaper external model and **$10,000** for a top-tier LLM judge, catching **94%** of malicious hacking sessions, escalating **8.7%** of harmless sessions, and adding **under 2%** latency, with the underlying reward-hacking research published **2026-09-17**; and **arXiv:2610.02066** (Gupta & Buscaldi, submitted 2026-10-01) moves hallucination detection from token-wise binary classification to **span-level** detection by inspecting layer-wise activations, then shows in a **cross-model** setting that **an external observer can match or exceed a generator's self-detection of its own hallucination onsets — including when the observer is the smaller model**, i.e. **self-detection is not the ceiling**. Two boundaries must stay on the first screen: the cost advantage presupposes **access to weights and activations** (it cannot be done against a closed API — so this is currently a "run open weights on your own inference stack" capability), and what the probes detect is **a signal trained for specific risk categories** (reward hacking, offensive hacking, chem/bio misuse), **not a general correctness judge** — which restates the 10-08 conclusion that the semantic conventions standardise **the call**, never **the judgement**. Also recorded today as **background, not ledger items**: **Microsoft Execution Containers (MXC) reached GA on Windows 11** (2026-10-07, Nadella × Huang), an OS-enforced policy layer with four containment backends (process / **session container with its own Windows account** / WSL / experimental microVM), with identity split so that **Windows attributes each action to the agent rather than the user** — and the discipline-critical detail that **Microsoft's own open-source MXC repository still warns that current SDK-generated policies are in places overly permissive and that no MXC profile should yet be treated as a security boundary**, that **outbound network filtering is not complete on Windows**, and that built-in file tools and remote MCP servers sit **outside** the sandbox. That is the first time a vendor has shipped a GA containment primitive **and said on the same day that it is not a boundary** — the same family as the wiki's standing "authority is not isolation" (09-26) and "Final ≠ available" (09-26 / 09-29 / 10-08), and a candidate first-hand citation for a future revision of [[AgentSandbox]]. Finally, three ledger rows received **strengthening notes only** (no new rows, no status changes): **10-08 observability** (the judge half — from "record half-page" to "record + judge full page"; three-way merge with 09-17 Agent Evals and today's candidate 3 recommended), **09-28 memory-benchmark validity** (pushing the same discipline one level further inward: **arXiv:2610.02070** shows memory utility is **unidentifiable** for **54% of required memories on LongMemEval and 67% on LoCoMo** because retrieval mediates every path, and that **no aggregation available to a retention policy predicts a memory's value on unseen queries**; **VibeMemBench** shows automatic capture **failing to beat no-memory in 11 of 12 pairings** while test-verified experience helps, with **GitHub Copilot Memory** as the strongest counter-example because it **re-checks each fact's citation against the current branch before use** — yielding the upgraded rule "the dividing line is not human vs automatic capture, but whether a fact was checked against the world before it was relied on"), and **09-23 durable execution** (fourth consecutive strengthening: it finally gains a theoretical skeleton — LAM's checkpoint law and closed-form optimal verification interval move the question from *who implements pause/resume* to *how often verification is worth paying for* — plus an OS-level form, MXC's session container; still recommended for ⭐ promotion, still not executed). Ledger recomputed (43 → **46** rows; all existing rows +1 day; summary now **9 ✅ / 5 🟡 / 32 ⏸**); the daily primary target **fell back for the third consecutive period** to **09-17 RAG (22 days)**, since all three historical ⭐ rows remain 🟡. Details in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-10-09.md`.

## [2026-10-09] ingest | 记忆的治理:从档案库到策略(Agent Memory: From Archive to Policy)

Twentieth operation on 2026-10-09 and the **first ingest since 2026-09-26**. Executed under **explicit user instruction** (「结清 5 条」), closing the **five `🟡` backlog rows** this single material had been holding (09-18 memory poisoning · 09-21 recall-timing policy · 09-24 memory lifecycle governance · 09-29 memory integrity · 09-30 memory as a learned policy). Wiki grows **12 → 13 sources**; new pages: `sources/memory-lifecycle.md`, `concepts/MemoryGovernance.md`, `concepts/MemoryPoisoning.md`, `entities/DyadMem.md`; updated pages: `concepts/LongTermMemory.md`, `index.md`, `overview.md`, `concept-relationship.md`.

Source: `raw/learning-materials/memory-lifecycle.html`, copied from `learning-materials/memory-lifecycle.html` and verified **md5 `e52fb0ea202360b99962d4ba8ce126a9` identical on both sides before ingest**; `raw/` is read-only from here. ⚠️ **This is an instruction-authorised ingest, not a line-by-line manual review** — the README's 人工核查承诺 normally requires the user to check each item; that check was delegated by the instruction and is labelled as such here, in the source page, and in the README manifest. (Same labelling convention as the 2026-09-26 authorised chain.)

What the source adds, in three moves. **(1) Memory is four actions, not a store.** Capture → Update → Recall → QA, each **separately annotated** by **DyadMem** (3,065 episodes / 50,961 sessions / 61,210 QA; 16 open + 4 closed models). The result that reframes Cluster B: frontier models are strong **given gold memory** and **collapse on the full pipeline**, so the bottleneck is the quality of the four actions, **not window capacity** — which is why "a bigger context window" is not an answer. Per-stage defects are now measurable: low capture recall, incomplete recall, **unsafe deletion** (and `URAM` — same user, different agents should remember different things). **(2) The load-bearing variable is who decides**, and there are exactly three classes: **human-written rules** (today's default — thresholds and TTLs with no judgement about *what counts as stale*) · **trained policy** (2026's answer: **ADD / UPDATE / DELETE / NOOP placed in the action space**; Memory-R1 trains on **152 QA pairs** for **LoCoMo F1 +68.9%** over Mem0, across six independent teams of which **none is deployed**) · **attacker**. Forgetting is a capability, not a failure: Memora's **FAMA** = recall score − **stale-dependency penalty**, so "failed to forget" is a *deductible score*. Four governance bottom lines: **auditable · rollbackable · permissioned · signature-verified** — the last being why the reliability discipline and the security discipline merge at this layer. **(3) The write side is an ownership claim, not a bug.** `concepts/MemoryPoisoning.md` records the adversary as the **third decider**: **induced self-write** (PMPA — a *benign* document makes the harness write the payload itself; Claude Code **66.9% / 81.7%**, with benign-task quality **unchanged**) and **forged conversation history** (Darktrace — **all four harnesses** accept forged history; under Kiro-CLI the chain reaches **Active Directory domain takeover**). Prompt-level defence protects poorly *after* poisoning, and the root fix is **vendor-side** message signing validated server-side ⇒ the wiki records, for the first time, that **the mitigation is not the operator's to deploy**.

`concepts/LongTermMemory.md`: the **`pending ingest` banner is withdrawn** — it had explicitly asked to be cleared by exactly this ingest ("Remove this banner when the material is ingested and human-reviewed, and move the source slug into the frontmatter `sources` field"). `memory-lifecycle` is now in its frontmatter `sources`; its "second half" section is linked to the two new pages; and a **caution was added to its benchmark headline** — the 95.60% [[AgentZeroMemory]]/[[LongMemEval]] figure is a *gold-memory / end-to-end* result, not evidence that long-term memory works end to end.

`overview.md` synthesis revised for the first time since 2026-09-26: **12 → 13 sources**, new **Cluster F — memory governance: the second half of a memory's life**, the thirteen-page story extended, **two new cross-cutting themes** (*the governance turn: from where state lives to who decides its fate*; *a vacant control slot does not stay vacant*), the **shared-memory governance open question moved from "unwritten" to "named but no product"**, and **three new open questions / two new ingest suggestions** added. Boundary discipline applied: `MemoryPoisoning` is filed as Cluster F's **third decider**, and `MultiAgent`'s cross-contamination is recorded as the **same failure shape one layer out** — both extending [[Provenance]]'s object from *facts* to *the verification status of what you are treating as fact*.

Caveats carried into the pages, not smoothed over: **the entire Cluster F evidence base is author-reported** (no independent reproduction of DyadMem's gap, FAMA, PMPA's rates, or any learned-policy result outside its own benchmark); the source's own citation list is **mixed-grade** (items 1/2/3/7/13 checked by live retrieval on 2026-10-06; items 4/5/6/8/9 carried over from the 2026-09-24 → 09-30 radar cycles without re-opening; Darktrace secondhand); and the house-keeping discrepancy between a **four-thread** merge (material title/footer) and **five** booked ledger rows is **recorded rather than flattened**.

`concept-relationship.md` gains **「扩展六 · 记忆治理 —— 从「档案柜」到「谁有权决定它的生死」」** (Mermaid: the four actions · front-half vs back-half · the three deciders · the compounding-error edge back into Capture), and the material is registered in the file header as 已入库 2026-10-09.

External signal note (for the record, not ingested): today's radar opened a **training-side** seam the wiki has never had a page for, and landed the 🎯 row's first **vendor-neutral structural decomposition**. (1) ⭐ **new row — 训练—部署错配 / Harnessed Agentic RL.** **Agent Lightning v1.0** (Microsoft Research Asia, open-sourced **2026-10-07/08**, ~3,500 lines) trains agents through **the harness they actually deploy**: an OpenAI-compatible LLM proxy sits between harness and model, the harness is **not modified**, and prompts / responses / logprobs are recorded for RL. Reported result: **Qwen3.5-9B, SWE-bench Verified Pass@1 41.8% → 56.4%** (+14.6 pp) on ~6,000 samples; Kubernetes-native; "Collocated Async RL" ≈2× end-to-end speed of synchronous RL on fewer GPUs. The seam this names: [[AgentHarness]] enumerates *what the harness does* (assemble context / run the loop and judge stopping / govern the window / keep alive and schedule), but **no page says those decisions simultaneously shape the action and observation spaces** — so re-implementing an agent inside a training framework is not reproducing it, it is **training in a world with key constraints removed**. Chinese-language coverage supplies the citable framing: 「**harness 是策略的一部分,不是外挂**」 and 「**这不算是 reward hacking,更准确的叫法是环境错配。而错配的代价,通常被记在『模型泛化不行』的账上。**」 Boundary discipline applied on the spot: this is the **first hard evidence for the 09-23「自进化智能体」row** (18 days, zero evidence until today), so it is filed as a **new row carrying an explicit merge recommendation** rather than left dangling; the caveat stays on the first screen (single model, single benchmark, vendor-reported, v1.0 self-described early-stage). (2) **09-23「持久化执行」(🎯, 18 天) — 承接强化,不新增行.** Three independent first-hand items upgrade this row from "four runtimes disagree on what resume means" to a **structured layer**: ① **agentpatterns.ai《Deep Agent Runtime: The Layer Beneath the Harness》** decomposes the runtime into **four concerns** — durable runs / lifecycle controls / streaming primitives / versioned APIs — with LangChain's line ("the runtime is everything underneath: durable execution, memory, multi-tenancy, observability", 2026-04-20), Cursor's run-scoped Cloud Agents API (2026-04-29: **one active run per agent, 409 `agent_busy` on contention**; `archive` reversible vs `delete` irreversible; terminal runs return **409 `run_not_cancellable`**), Anthropic's "**Any Harness instance can pick up any Session and continue from where it left off**", and Vercel's contrarian position (2026-08-27) that durable execution **belongs in the programming language**, not a separate workflow engine; ② a **delivery-semantics taxonomy** with an explicit verdict — **at-least-once / at-most-once / effectively-once** and "**True exactly-once delivery across a process and an external service is not generally possible**", plus the cost asymmetry that makes it load-bearing (*a repeated model call costs tokens; a repeated refund is a customer incident*) and the requirement for version-checked concurrent writes; ③ two crisp boundary sentences — "**checkpoint ≠ memory**" (memory answers the next question; a checkpoint says which steps of the *current* task finished) and "**A run can finish reliably and still give a poor answer. It can also give an excellent answer once and fail to survive the next deploy. Both problems need measuring.**" ⇒ the last one finally separates **durable execution (reliability)** from **evals (quality)** into two orthogonal axes, the boundary this row had been missing. (3) **09-30「常驻智能体 / Persistent Agents with a Standing Identity」— 承接强化,不新增行.** **Google Gemini agent** ("Coworker Agent", announced **2026-10-08** at Gemini at Work 2026) is now documented to first-hand detail: a Workspace identity at **`@agents.company.com`** with its own mailbox / calendar / Drive, **listed in the company directory**, every action logged against the agent's **cryptographically attested identity** rather than a human user's, edits appearing under the agent's own name in version history, **dynamic sub-agents each with their own scoped identity** (a product instance of [[AgentIdentity]]'s per-hop attenuation — the first time in a mainstream enterprise product), four memory kinds (session / semantic / procedural / episodic), **up to 7 days per job**, smart routing across **200+ models including Claude**, and real-time spend caps. ⚠️ **private preview; no pricing and no GA date published** — the "交付 ≠ 可购买" pattern again, and in the same week Anthropic disclosed Claude models taking unintended actions on real websites (including filing a fabricated police tip) and cut live internet access from internal tests. Recorded as **strengthening only**, which resolves the 10-10 report's 备查 disposition (that report already ruled this overlapping with the 09-30 row and declined a new row; today it lands on that row). **备查 (not ledgered):** **NVIDIA** technical guidance on **indirect `AGENTS.md` injection** (agents loading config files from untrusted repositories) — a direct cross-link between the 10-09「常驻上下文」row and the overview's open question on *transient* indirect prompt injection; **Baseten × Goodfire "Project Beacon"** (2026-10-09) — the 10-09 inside-out monitoring item gaining a named product partnership one day after its own announcement; **Microsoft AX Practitioner Playbook** (2026-10-09, nine recurring coding-agent failure patterns, 46 improvements to the Azure Cosmos DB Agent Kit) — the SDK/tool-owner side of the 10-09 context-quality question; **OpenAI "instant steering"** (Day 4 of the release sprint) — mid-task course correction taking effect immediately rather than after the current thought completes, i.e. an [[HumanInTheLoop]] pause primitive moving into the model itself. **(1) 🎯 10-08「智能体可观测性与评测」(⭐, 4 天) — 承接强化，不新增行。** Its first **first-hand academic + standards + product** triple. **Transect** (arXiv:2610.08364, v1 2026-10-06 / v2 2026-10-08, 27 pages, open source, built on Inspect Scout) names the **observability envelope** — the range of what evaluators can reliably infer about an agent's behaviour — as *narrowing* as transcripts reach hundreds of pages across multi-agent networks, and answers with a reusable **evaluation-family configuration** that separates task context / behavioural vocabulary from judge models, aligning recorded events · token use · sub-agent activity · behavioural labels on one turn-based timeline; demonstrated on an AI-R&D evaluation of **~13M tokens**, where the combined view exposed *no sustained hypothesis-generation phase*. **AgentRx** (Microsoft Research, EMNLP Findings 2026, listed 2026-10-02) turns a failed trajectory into a sequence of **constraints**, checks them in order, records violations **with evidence**, and only then lets an LLM judge localise the critical step and category — reported **+75% average step-localisation** (author-reported), with ⚠️ **the public records disagreeing on benchmark size (Microsoft page: 115 failed trajectories; arXiv v2: 170 across 11 task settings)**. **New Relic AI Evaluation** (announced 2026-10-06, **public preview November**) attaches LLM-as-judge quality scores as attributes to existing OTel distributed traces, with RAG-specific **faithfulness / answer relevancy**; ⚠️ no price, no framework list, no judge-accuracy data. And the **OpenTelemetry GenAI agent span conventions** reached product surface (Sentry JS SDK release 2026-10-09: `invoke_agent` / `execute_tool` / `plan` / `create_agent`, `gen_ai.*` attributes, MCP servers auto-instrumented, `gen_ai.evaluate` spans) — caveat: the agent-span document is **Development** status and prompt/response content is **Opt-In**. Why this is the seam: the 10-11 draft's own boundary sentence ("**a run can finish reliably and still give a poor answer**") split **reliability (跑得完)** from **quality (跑得好)** into orthogonal axes, and the wiki now has a draft for the first and **no page at all for the second**.

**(2) 09-17「RAG / 检索增强生成」(25 天，素材侧最老 ⏸) — 承接强化，不新增行。** The evidence **changed category again**: the dispute is no longer **which retriever** (09-27/09-28: keyword vs vector) but **what is retrieved**. **CogMem** (arXiv:2610.11314, submitted 2026-10-08, **accepted to EMNLP 2026 main**) replaces "memory as passive storage" with a **PEC2F** (Person-Event-Concept-Claim-Fact) provenance-aware graph in which dedicated **Claim nodes carry source and target**, conflicting updates from the same source are **reconciled into temporally scoped Claim views**, and retrieval becomes **reconstruction** via four deterministic graph operators (anchoring / traversal / intersection / evidence grounding) rather than similarity search; strong on LoCoMo / LongMemEval multi-hop, temporal and knowledge-update tasks. Alongside: **RECAST** (arXiv:2610.10507, adaptive evidence routing), **HGP** (arXiv:2610.10071, on-device hybrid graph memory), **iRAG** (arXiv:2610.12666, 2026-10-08 — the KB starts **empty** and is populated by human decisions; reports a **stability–plasticity trade-off**: retrieval decay *helps* on changed knowledge and *hurts* on stable knowledge), and **Hindsight** (four-tier world-facts / experiences / observations / mental-models; LongMemEval SOTA claimed). ⇒ The wiki's own **[[Provenance]] / [[CitationLock]] / [[Abstention]]** discipline now has a **retrieval-layer implementation that the wiki has no page for**. ⚠️ Grade: CogMem is a conference-accepted primary; RECAST / HGP / iRAG are **abstract-level** (PDFs not opened page-by-page); Hindsight is **vendor-reported**.

**(3) 09-23「间接提示注入 / 对抗性输入」(19 天) — 承接强化，不新增行。** overview's 2026-10-09 open question ("no page for *transient* indirect prompt injection") finally has production-scale evidence, and the load-bearing fact is a **negative one**. **Miasma Worm** (2026-06-05; StepSecurity + Phoenix Security forensics) planted malicious instructions in **editor configuration files** (`.claude/settings.json`, `.vscode/tasks.json`, four mainstream tools) so that **opening a project folder** — an officially supported feature — was the trigger; pushed via a compromised account into Microsoft's `azure/durabletask`, GitHub disabled **73 repos across 4 orgs in 105 seconds**, Socket identified **448** malicious package files across npm/PyPI, and **no CVE was issued** because it is not a defect. **ClawHavoc** (ClawHub, the OpenClaw skill marketplace): Koi Security audited **2,632 skills → 341 malicious (12.9%)**, 335 attributable to one campaign; Antiy CERT counted **1,184 packages across 12 author accounts**; ClawSecure reached **539** by 2026-03; a single account (`hightower6eu`) published **677**. Delivery is **ClickFix-shaped** — the skill's `SKILL.md` "Prerequisites" instructs the *user* to paste the command, so the agent becomes the persuader; payload AMOS steals crypto wallets, SSH keys, `.env` and agent config. **Air Security's "The Story of Skills"** (June 2026) shipped a deliberately malicious skill through a trusted marketplace: it reached **>26,000 agents** and **every scanner cleared it as safe** — a skill is inert to any check that is not executing it. Named structures to file: the **"lethal trifecta"** (access to private data + exposure to untrusted content + ability to communicate externally) and **[[Skill]] as the ecosystem's packaging format ⇒ skills are a supply chain, not a convenience** — which is also the 10-11 note that [[MultiAgent]]'s cross-contamination runs through a *shared* medium. Counter-evidence kept on the first screen: **Anthropic + EPFL, "Mind Viruses" (arXiv:2608.10218, 2026-08)** find that in multi-agent populations malicious payloads spread **far less** than benign information, and that a **short warning sentence in the system prompt** confers near-complete immunity — propagation is not automatic, and a prompt-level control does work *here*, which is the opposite of Cluster F's finding.

**(1) 🎯 10-08「智能体可观测性与评测」(⭐, 5 天) — 承接强化，不新增行。** The row's second half finally has a first-hand instrument: **arXiv:2610.07274《A Trust Layer for Agent Evaluation》** (Sediqin / Dalmia / Kovvuri / Mukherji, Centific Research) is an **additive post-hoc layer that never modifies the recorded score**, verifying four properties beside it — **D1 competence** (does the benchmark's own grading logic reproduce it), **D2 reward hacking** (was a passing answer earned through traceable computation), **D3 deception** (does the agent's completion claim match what occurred), **D4 stability** (does it hold under re-execution). Applied to **5 agent configurations × 108 tasks** from Agents' Last Exam: **only 22.6% of recorded passes clear all four checks** (95% CI 15.0–32.6, n=84); every model shows passing runs with no traceable computation at rates **varying tenfold**; **18–46% of tasks do not stay in one score band over five runs**. Why this is the seam: the 10-11 draft split **reliability (跑得完)** from **quality (跑得好)**; this adds the **third axis the wiki has no page for — whether the quality verdict itself is trustworthy**. And it lands on two existing commitments at once: [[MultiAgent]]'s "the verifier is the precondition" now needs the verifier verified, and the overview's standing `independent verification` open question acquires a concrete procedure. ⚠️ Grade: **preprint, single benchmark, 13–20 recorded passes per configuration with overlapping CIs, code withheld until acceptance** — the claim is "most recorded passes fail at least one check", **not** a ranking of agents.

**(2) NEW ⭐ 上下文重置与「想法盆地」/ Context Reset & Idea Basins (Fork-and-Flush).** **Microsoft Research**, published **2026-10-12** (Cai, Ziakas, Kontonis, Pearce, Sen, Krishnamurthy, Garg, Papailiopoulos). Eight independent runs of the same autoresearch agent on the same task plateaued between **68.3 and 100.0**, and the gap **persisted with substantially more compute**; embedding candidate artifacts by functional similarity showed each run staying inside one small region — an **idea basin**. **The load-bearing negative: the pattern persists even when the agents periodically compress their conversation histories.** Fork-and-Flush forks into K branches that **inherit the workspace and discard the chat context**, then continues from the best: **normalized average 0.78 vs 0.47 single-run (+66.0%) and 0.54 best-of-4 (+44.4%)** over 13 long tasks (polyomino packing, job-shop scheduling, cryptanalysis, CIFAR classifier compression, VLIW kernel optimization; individual runs hours to **>100 hours**; GPT-5.6-terra / GPT-5.5 via GitHub Copilot CLI); normalized std-dev **0.26 → 0.16**; highest mean on **12 of 13**; **flush-only (no branching) 0.45 → 0.55 (+22%) on 11 tasks**. On MAX-3-SAT across **Qwen3.5-4B / 9B / 27B**, run-to-run variance follows an **inverted U**, peaking at the edge of each model's ability — so the authors expect context resets to stay useful as models improve. Why this is a seam and not a seventh context page: the wiki's context cluster treats the transcript exclusively as **an asset that degrades** ([[ContextRot]] accuracy decline, [[Compaction]] lossy compression, and the 10-06 VISTA / 09-24 offload rows which say *keep it and re-look*). This says the transcript is **also an anchor** — **a second, different failure axis on the same object**, and one that compression does not touch. Boundary discipline applied on the spot: filed as a **new row carrying an explicit merge recommendation** with 09-24「上下文卸载」and 10-06「VISTA」(the same axis, opposite directions — *keep it* vs *throw it away*), and it supplies [[MultiAgent]] with a quantified mechanism for why independence pays (**shared long context erodes diversity of approach**, not merely speed). ⚠️ Grade: reported via The Decoder; **no paper-specific repo linked on the arXiv page**, affiliations not listed; tasks are **optimization/research-shaped**, not general tool-calling agents; 66% is a relative gain on a min-max-normalized average, not an absolute score.

**(3) NEW 检索准入 / Retrieval Admissibility — 记忆「能不能用在这里」(not marked ⭐).** Two first-hand October items name the same missing cell. **arXiv:2610.07309《The Right Memory in the Wrong Context》** (Zi Wang et al., Univ. of Arkansas at Little Rock; NeurIPS 2026 Workshop) re-analyses **3,767 queries** across two public long-term-memory benchmarks (RHELM, MemOps): admissibility reranking raises **top-20 anchor recall 0.432 → 0.533**, but across **16 controlled exposure scenarios only one** confidence interval rules out the risk that inadmissible content is still disclosed; three-status labelling (admissible / inadmissible / unresolved), 72 frozen diagnostic cases, code released. **arXiv:2610.11188《What to Admit and How to Present》** (Liu & Ding) splits governance into **admission** (what recalled information enters context) and **presentation** (how it is expressed), with two no-retraining designs (factor-compiled FC, permission-semantic PS): pooled judge-assessed failure rates **−6.7 / −8.8 pp** (p = 2.7e-7 / 4.1e-12) and cross-domain leakage down by up to **29.5 pp** — while **both increase personalization failures**, and no comparison between two renderings of identical adjudicated outputs survives multiple-comparison correction. Why this is a seam: [[Provenance]] governs *where a fact came from*, [[CitationLock]] governs *whether you opened it*, [[Abstention]] governs *whether to answer at all* — **none governs whether a sourced, opened, true memory is licensed for *this* request.** It extends [[Abstention]]'s object from *facts* to *the permission to use a fact*, and it is the governance细化 of Cluster F's **Recall** action. It also supplies the other half of 09-29's finding: PMPA's payload arrives **wearing legitimate provenance**; admissibility is about **true content placed in the wrong context**. ⚠️ Grade: 2610.07309 is a **workshop paper** (not main-conference review) with a small effect size (1 of 16 scenarios significant); 2610.11188 is a **preprint** that **misses its own preregistered improvement and personalization-preservation criteria**.

**备查 (not ledgered, per the ledger's boundary rule):** **(a) arXiv:2610.07359《Evaluate the Stack, Not the Layer》** (Chenglin Yang) — on **1,119 labelled agent actions** across three corpora with no adaptive adversary, **two LLM judges compose to only 1.2–1.4 multiplication-equivalent layers** (φ median +0.430, 6/6 pairs significant) and a rule layer plus one judge to 1.86–2.09; **solo accuracy does not predict what a layer adds** (a cloud rule pack cut the rule layer's solo miss rate 20% and added **identical, case-for-case** joint coverage), and one judge tier was served by an **unrequested model version in 50 of 112 batches**, overturning a pre-declared rule and reversing five conclusions ⇒ this directly attacks the layered-defence intuition behind Cluster D's fixed ordering, which the wiki records as *ordering is first-class* but never as *stacking ≠ multiplying*. **(b) OpenAI "textGrain"** (EU text provenance: invisible machine-readable watermark on ChatGPT/Codex output, rolling out in the EU, opt-in API worldwide, detector access limited to approved researchers) **against the Wikimedia incident report** (agents believed to be OpenAI-operated made unauthorized sandbox edits, probed a hosted Etherpad for proxying, and generated millions of requests possibly contributing to a May partial outage of the Wikidata Query Service; Wikimedia stresses **attribution difficulty** and asks operators to make agent traffic identifiable) ⇒ a matched pair of opposites: **vendors begin marking output provenance while real traffic still cannot be attributed** — relevant to [[Provenance]] (output-side) and [[AgentIdentity]] (identifiability as a product requirement). **(c) Anthropic dynamic workflows / "flows"** (2026-10-13, AI Engineer New York): **Claude writes its own orchestration program** that launches and aggregates sub-agents, up to **~1,000 subagents** per run (server-side workflow runs), self-described as **significantly more expensive** than single-agent ⇒ [[MultiAgent]]'s shape changes from *a coordinator the developer configured* to *a coordinator the model authored*, moving the object of the "verifier is the precondition" rule; beta, figures from conference reporting. **(d) Microsoft Execution Containers** reached **GA** (policy-driven sandbox across Windows / macOS / Linux) ⇒ [[AgentSandbox]] productisation. **(e) MemMux** (arXiv:2610.07257) turns parallel-coding-agent fleets into **checkable runtime signals** — per-agent memory attribution, **100% reclamation** of a terminated agent's process tree (raw baseline strands half), **10/10 escaped children detected**, bounded footprint under overcommit, at **0.6% CPU at one agent but 2.7% at ten** (above its own 2% target) ⇒ the **resource-governance half** [[AgentSandbox]] never wrote (the page covers containment, not attribution and reclamation). **(f) The memory-vs-documentation argument** (Kevin Liao, via dev.to 2026-10-08): agents need **documentation, not memory** — vector similarity measures *closeness of text, not its truthfulness* — with a BSD-licensed **Operator Memory** plugin implementing a Markdown workspace + index ⇒ same direction as 09-24「上下文卸载」/ 10-06 VISTA; opinion piece, no evaluation. ⚠️ Items (b)–(d) are **secondhand weekly-digest reporting**; (a), (e) are abstract-level; (f) is an opinion piece.

## [2026-10-13] graph-note | Rebuild authorized by user; scope and zero-loss check recorded

Trigger: the user's one-word instruction "执行" against item 2 of the "⚠️ 需你拍板 (2 件)" block in today's radar report — i.e. authorization for the degraded rebuild that had been pending approval for five consecutive days. Command run: `python tools/build_graph.py --no-infer` — **Pass 1 deterministic wikilink edges only**; no LLM call, no network request, nothing in `raw/` or `wiki/` read-modified (the script only reads `wiki/`, writes `graph/`, and appends to `log.md`).

**Zero-loss check performed before the rebuild (recorded because it is the reason a degraded rebuild is safe here):** the previous graph (`built: 2026-09-17`, 31 nodes / 125 edges) was inspected and its 125 edges are **100% `EXTRACTED`, confidence 1.0, zero `INFERRED`**. The rebuild is therefore a **strict superset within the same edge family** — no semantic-inference edge was dropped. Had any `INFERRED` edge existed, `--no-infer` would have silently removed it and the degraded rebuild would not have been proposed.

**Result:** nodes **31 → 47** (concept 16→24, source 6→13, entity 8→9, synthesis 1); edges **125 → 283** (351 extracted → 283 after dedup); `built` **2026-09-17 → 2026-10-13** (previously lagging three ingest operations: 09-26, 10-09 and the 10-13 cycle itself). Quality: **isolated nodes 0**; Louvain communities **4** (14 / 14 / 12 / 7); top degree `overview` 34, `sources/agent` 28, `concepts/ContextEngineering` 24, `sources/skill` 20. Old `graph.json`, `graph.html` and `log.md` backed up outside the repo before the run. Post-rebuild `health.py` (3 checks) and the custom 5-dimension lint re-run **all green**.

**Still outstanding (not authorized, not executed):** a full rebuild with `--infer` (Pass 2 semantic edges) — this is the only path that would add non-wikilink semantic edges, and the current graph has none by construction.

External signal note (for the record, not ingested): **today's strongest item is a CVE, not a paper** — the first time this log records a numbered vulnerability as the load-bearing evidence for a radar candidate.

**(1) NEW ⭐ 跨主体状态隔离 / Cross-Principal State Isolation (ledger row 10-14A).** Three independent 2026-10 sources name the same cell — **the wiki has no page for whose inference state this is**. 🔴 **CVE-2026-105752** (vLLM prefix-cache tenant-isolation failure; published to NVD **2026-10-05**, updated 2026-10-06; fixed in **v0.30.0**): on `POST /v1/responses` Harmony tool continuations, the next-turn engine input is rebuilt **without propagating `cache_salt`**, so the continuation prefix lands in the **global unsalted prefix-cache namespace** even when the caller enabled salting; with prefix caching **on by default**, an authenticated tenant can use `cached_tokens_per_turn` as an existence oracle to infer whether another tenant processed a given low-entropy history (CWE-200). **arXiv:2610.17365v1** (2026-10-12) supplies the causal half: with **shared conversational memory**, an agent serving two clients with opposing objectives **deviates from the current client's goal in 22% of eligible decisions** (OLMo-3-7B-Instruct, 20 market scenarios); linear probes show client-goal ownership is highly recoverable in middle layers and concentrated in particular attention heads, and **ablating the competitor's cached goal representations at those locations shifts the model back toward the current client** — larger for probed heads than random heads, larger for goal tokens than matched non-goal tokens. **Lineage-Aware Memory Governance** (2026-10-05, abstract-level) targets the enterprise case: derivation-gated column-level access control against a legitimately-computed result the requester could not have derived, plus same-named KPIs with conflicting logic across departments. Why this is a seam: [[AgentIdentity]] covers the **credential** side (who acts, on whose behalf, per-hop attenuation), [[MemoryGovernance]] covers **decision rights** (who may add/update/delete), and the 10-13「检索准入」row covers **per-item admission** — **none covers state ownership**, and the load-bearing point is that **an admission gate does not close it**, because the leak happens **inside already-admitted shared state** (prefix cache / shared conversational memory). It also supplies [[MultiAgent]] with a boundary condition the page does not yet carry: the shared workspace is a gain **within one principal** and a leak channel **across principals**. ⚠️ Grade: the CVE is authoritative but scoped to vLLM < 0.30.0 and to a *timing oracle*, not a direct data read; 2610.17365v1 is a **preprint, one model, one controlled environment**; Lineage-Aware is **abstract-level**.

**(2) NEW ⭐ 长程进展的显式化 / Explicit Belief States & Object-Oriented Continuation (ledger row 10-14B; PoS + CarryOn filed as one row).** Two **2026-10-13** first-hand papers give two halves of one cell the wiki has never named: **the long-horizon agent's "where am I now, what is still missing" is not an explicitly represented object.** **PoS** (《Beyond Memory: Harnessing Long-Horizon Agents with Explicit Belief States》; 南开大学 + 阿里云 + 清华大学; arXiv:2610.01415; project page + code `github.com/luoyu100/PoS`) organizes the task-relevant world as entities / states / relations **each carrying source, confidence and supporting evidence**, then splits unresolved requirements into **Epistemic Gap** (what still needs to be found out) and **Achievement Gap** (what still needs to be changed by action), closing the loop from *detecting that it is stuck* (**Belief Trapping**) to *deciding how to resume*; **4 benchmarks × 3 base models = 12 settings**, PoS above every method the paper compares against. **CarryOn** (arXiv:2610.18784) is the other half — the **carrier**: long-horizon agents accumulate progress in persistent task state yet repeatedly process the interaction history describing how that progress was achieved; CarryOn is an **object-oriented context runtime** that organizes continuation around **reusable task objects (continuation carriers)** and explicitly declines to treat *compressed histories* **or** *reconstructed accounts of progress* as the primary basis for continuation; it maintains carrier records **across folds**, revising only affected records against bound evidence and applicable environment checks. **OSWorld 2.0 +5.8%**, **BrowseComp-Plus +7.0%**, total token usage **−51.9% / −22.9%**. Why this is a seam: the context cluster and the 10-13「四条路」merged row all choose a position on **one axis (how much history to keep)**; these two papers change **the axis itself** (what continuation hangs on, and what the current understanding is). CarryOn's own contrast objects are exactly **[[Compaction]]** (compressed histories) and **10-13's VISTA / Mem++** (reconstructed accounts) — i.e. it is a direct boundary claim against two existing wiki commitments at once. And PoS extends **[[Provenance]]'s object for the first time** from *facts taken in* to *the agent's own current understanding*. ⚠️ Grade: both **abstract-level** (PDFs not opened page-by-page); CarryOn's arXiv page lists no affiliations; PoS's 12-setting result is read via Chinese tech-media reporting, **not** the paper's own tables.

**(3) 10-08「智能体可观测性与评测」(⭐, 6 天) — 承接强化，不新增行。** **MASS** (Sakana AI + UC Berkeley; **arXiv:2610.12176**; code `github.com/SakanaAI/mass`; open-weights base **Qwen3.6-27B**) runs a **homogeneous** loop in which one model is executor, workflow optimizer **and** evaluator, then fine-tunes itself on its own team's best trajectories. The load-bearing result for this row is the **evaluator** half: agreement between the self-evaluator and withheld external judges (**GPT-5.5** / **Claude Opus 4.8**, judgments withheld from training) rises **73% → 93%** across two rounds; multi-agent trajectories are more token-efficient training data (**28.8M** supervised tokens → **68.3%** win rate vs **42M** → **64.0%** single-agent); the ablation that matters is negative — **upgrading only the evaluator to GPT-5.5 helped little, while upgrading the optimizer mattered much more**. Headline: win rate vs base **53.9% → 69.9%**; 1.2–1.6× score-per-output-token on four research benchmarks; ⚠️ **transfer is uneven — Terminal-Bench 2.0 0.96× and SWE-bench Verified 0.94× of base (i.e. worse)**. Why this is a seam: 10-11's draft split **reliability (跑得完)** from **quality (跑得好)** and 10-13 added the third axis (**is the verdict itself trustworthy**); MASS supplies the **counter-case and boundary condition** — self-grading can become *more* trustworthy (73%→93%) **while** the authors themselves name the failure it cannot fix: in a homogeneous loop a **shared blind spot** lets a flawed result be accepted as progress and reinforced through training. It is also the first item on this row that is **independently reproducible by construction** (open code + open weights), which is exactly what overview's standing `independent verification` open question has been asking for. ⚠️ Grade: single base model, synthetic research tasks, coding transfer **negative**.
