External signal note (for the record, not ingested): today's three radar findings **all landed on credentials / authority / runtime infrastructure rather than on model behaviour or capability**, and every one of them is a **specification in the process of being written** rather than a published or reproduced result. Two of the three rest on IETF **individual** Internet-Drafts — a provenance grade this wiki keeps in a separate column from vendor self-reports, and one that here carries its own explicit self-declared limits (single implementer; same-process verifier; no defence against a malicious host operator). Recorded because it is the first radar day whose load-bearing evidence is **institutional process rather than paper or product**.

**(1) NEW ⭐ 委派链的可验证性 —— 路径上没有授权服务器的那一跳 / Verifiable Delegation Without an Authorization Server (AIP).** The seam is one sentence: [[AgentIdentity]]'s per-hop attenuation **assumes an issuing authority is present at every hop** — under RFC 8693 the narrowing is done by an authorization server / token service that evaluates policy and re-issues a narrower token, and AIP's own text states the invariant being broken: "*The holder does not narrow its own token. Some hops have no such server.*" **AIP** (`draft-prakash-aip-02`, individual Internet-Draft, dated **2026-10-10**, intended status Standards Track, expires 2027-04-13, single author) covers exactly those hops: one agent subdelegating to another, or delegating across organizations with no arranged trust. It supplies a delegation-chain model (authority link L0 signed by the issuer root key, delegation links L1..LN, N = delegation depth), **one attenuation invariant**, and a **normative verification algorithm** (Step V1 chain integrity onward) letting a relying party answer, from the token and the issuer's public key and **without calling back to the origin**, who granted the authority / through which chain / and that **no hop widened what it received**. Two encodings: a JWT chain in which each link names the next holder's key, and a **Biscuit** token with Datalog policy; bindings given for **MCP**, **A2A** and generic HTTP. Neighbour: **Agent Delegation Receipts** (`draft-nelson-agent-delegation-receipts-11`, **2026-10-09**, 29 pp.) records what a *human* principal authorized — scope, hard boundaries, validity window, and a **hash of the operator's stated instructions**, all signed; -11 replaced its custom signature with a **JWS (RFC 7515)** profile over **JCS-canonicalized JSON (RFC 8785)** plus a **COSE_Sign1 (RFC 9052)** profile, and adds multi-agent scope-attenuation rules and a revocation registry with **cascade semantics**. Its most citable part is the author's own OAuth-WG post (2026-10-10): one reference implementation and it is his own ⇒ single-implementer, same-process verifier, **no control against a malicious operator of the agent host**, individual draft with no IETF standing; he maps it against Google AP2 signed mandates, Decagon PACT, ERC-8004 and UCANs. The counter-evidence must sit beside the conclusion: **Poppy / Personal Agent Protocol draft 0.1** (Meta + Sierra, announced 2026-10-06, draft 10-09) puts four mechanical checks on the *connection* (discovery doc, client id + key, issuer match with OAuth metadata, DPoP-bound token) while the *authorization* layer is two scopes plus per-company `custom_scopes` — "**the token proves the agent, not the errand**". Timing evidence that this cell is live now: IETF 127 (San Francisco, 2026-11-14 → 11-20) has an approved SEC-Area BOF, "**Agent Use of Delegation and Interaction Traceability (audit)**". 🔴 Grade: all three are individual Internet-Drafts — replaceable, obsolete-able, no standing; the Poppy reading is a third-party commentary, not the draft text. **Not ingested.**

**(2) NEW ⭐ 写入侧的弃权 —— 一条门槛管不住所有类别 / Write-Side Abstention (Category-Conditioned Retention).** [[Abstention]] today is an **exit-side** discipline (no sourced evidence ⇒ refuse to *answer*); [[MemoryGovernance]] splits memory into four actions and asks **who decides**; [[Provenance]] asks **where it came from**. No page asks **whether it should be stored at all**, nor admits that **one global threshold cannot serve every category**. **arXiv:2610.07100** (Owolabi / Gupta / Fei Wang, submitted **2026-10-05**) measures the asymmetry on a deployed cold-start memory pipeline with 100 synthetic personas: across **4,715** candidate assertions, only **77.9%** of value/belief assertions are supported by their source versus **96.2%** for all other categories — and a **global confidence threshold cannot separate these** (it either admits unsupported value claims or discards well-evidenced ones). Conditioning the bar on category (stricter bar on values alone) reduces unsupported retentions **6.2% → 4.0%** (~36% relative, consistent across folds) while preserving an estimated **13 percentage points more coverage** (95% CI 9.8–16.0) than a global threshold at comparable retention — framed as selective prediction at the **write boundary**. Its read-side twin arrived the same week: **MemoType / TriMEM** (arXiv:2610.11573, 2026-10-08) reports that any **single** retrieval strategy faces a fundamental upper bound on expected precision in multi-class corpora, and routes instead (+16.18% Recall@1). Same criterion, both ends. **DAEDALUS** (arXiv:2610.08048v1, 2026-10-06) supplies the executable form of "verify before admitting": explorer-generated tasks with no oracle verifier, heuristics accepted only after repeated success (+15.9 pts mean success rate, up to 2.2× pass⁵). Boundary: the Gate extension worth keeping is that [[MemoryGovernance]]'s **third decider (the attacker) exploits exactly this gap** — a legitimately-sourced but insufficiently-supported value assertion reused as established fact *is* PMPA's payload wearing valid provenance, now with a number attached. 🔴 Grade: single preprint, synthetic personas, effect modest but consistent; MemoType and DAEDALUS read at abstract level only. **Not ingested.**

**(3) NEW 外壳贡献的可测量化 —— 模型不动、只换外壳能差多少 / Quantifying the Harness's Contribution.** [[AgentHarness]] asserts `Agent = Model + Harness` and lists four duties, but the wiki has **no number separating the harness's contribution from the model's** — "the harness matters" has been an assertion, not a measurement. **Learn2Play Bench** (arXiv:2610.08215, NUS; v1 **2026-10-06**, v2 10-08) supplies one: 20 text games whose rules are novel or counterintuitive (existing benchmarks put the rules in the prompt, so you cannot tell on-the-spot learning from recalled pretraining), 9 backbones × 6 experience-retention methods × 2 harness swaps, plus 21–30 human students per game. Finding (3) is the one for this page: **with the backbone fixed, changing the harness can improve performance while reducing estimated inference cost** — Claude Code + Opus 5 Max **81.8** vs OpenCode **74.5**; Codex + GPT-5.6-SOL **74.3** vs **57.0** (7–17 point swings), and OpenCode + Kimi K3 uses **56% fewer input tokens** per episode than the raw-memory method while scoring higher Max. Finding (1) bears on the context/memory cluster: **retaining complete records of actions and feedback supports learning better than summarizing experience into rules** — *but the ordering reverses on a different backbone* (Memory leads all four metrics under Kimi K3; EvoTest leads Max under Opus 5), so which carrier wins is a property of the model, not a universal rule. The failure worth recording for the memory pages: in one GemForge run **EvoTest turned a single failed fusion into a prompt rule banning a combination it had never tried** — over-generalization hardened into a durable rule, which is neither [[MemoryPoisoning]] (no adversary) nor staleness (the world did not move). Finding (2): peak humans still beat agents, repeat actions less (similarity 0.54 vs 0.66) and set new personal bests after setbacks more often (33% vs 22%). Corroborating but **second-hand throughout**: a 2026-10-14 roundup of the "harness cluster" (LongHarness arXiv:2610.13075 — frozen model, GLM-5.3-Flash exact match 4.4% → 61.1%, harness beats Claude Code by 12.1 and Codex by 18.9; Memento 3 arXiv:2610.11794 — keep a compiled rulebook only if replay reproduces every observed transition, clears all 25 public ARC-AGI-3 games at 44% of human actions; Evo² arXiv:2610.13025 Google DeepMind — 48.3% SWE-bench Verified vs 41.0% baseline). 🔴 Grade: Learn2Play's fine-grained numbers were read via second-hand write-ups and **not verified against the paper's tables**; only the three qualitative findings and the design are abstract-confirmed; the cluster roundup is a blog aggregator. Boundary note worth carrying into [[AgentHarness]]: **any score must be reported together with the harness that produced it** — a model-only leaderboard is not comparable across harnesses. **Not ingested.**

External signal note (for the record, not ingested): **today is the first radar day whose strongest evidence is an attack that defeats the wiki's own load-bearing ordering claim.** Cluster D's chain — `propose → resolve → authorize → gate → execute`, with "ordering is a first-class design object" recorded as a cross-cutting theme since 2026-09-24 — asserts an order. Today's candidate 1 shows six mainstream orchestration layers dispatching the call **before** the model's refusal arrived, i.e. **the order is a property of the implementation, not of the design.** Candidates 2 and 3 are the same shape one layer out: authorization **inherited** across a protocol boundary, and a control (memory) **owned by a party the wiki never listed**.

**(1) NEW ⭐ LLM—编排层—工具的信任边界 / The LLM–Orchestrator–Tool Trust Boundary (ledger row 10-16A).** **ToFu** (*ToFu: Exploiting and Fuzzing the LLM–Orchestrator–Tool Trust Boundary*; **arXiv:2610.13651v1**; Penn State + Université Montpellier + Dartmouth; accepted at **FSE 2027**, Shenzhen) names a participant the wiki has never had a page for: **the orchestration layer** — the thing that "translates model outputs into structured tool invocations". Two defaults it breaks at once, both load-bearing for Cluster D: **(a) model output = intent**, and **(b) a tool enforces the semantics it advertises**. Its attack, **"Repeat-After-Me"**, is trivial: knowing the tool-call format the orchestrator recognises, the attacker writes text naming a target tool plus its own arguments and asks the LLM to repeat it verbatim — the model "merely reproduces the supplied text" and the orchestrator dispatches it. Reproduced on **six** frameworks: **LangGraph, Microsoft Agent Framework, AutoGen, Google ADK 2.0, AWS Strands Agents, CrewAI**. The LangGraph 1.2.0 + Llama 3.1 8B transcript is the load-bearing one: first attempt — model refuses but *prints the call as text*, nothing executes; reframed as hypothetical and told to emit only the quoted text — the model returns it in structured form, **LangGraph executes it immediately with the attacker's arguments, and the model's refusal arrives only after the tool returned.** The authors' sentence is the page's title: "**The refusal text and the executed call disagree: the system prompt shaped what the model said, not what the orchestrator did.**" Missing pre-execution check reported to the LangGraph maintainers. Second leg, at ecosystem scale: ToFu derives semantic checks from each tool's own metadata + a CWE-guided sanitizer corpus (74 sanitizers / 21 introspection tracers) and fuzzes **273 MCP servers / 3,405 tools** ⇒ **253 verified bugs in 343 tools across 118 servers** (from 508 candidate flags): reflected output **115** (CWE-116/79) · path traversal **64** (CWE-22) · log injection **58** (CWE-117) · **SSRF 7** (CWE-918) · data exfiltration **5** (CWE-201) · **OS command injection 3** (CWE-78) · leftover temp file 1. Their diagnosis: "**many tools implicitly rely on the LLM to sanitize inputs**". The independent reading (Vibhum Dubey, quoted by CSO): "**Model output is not the permission**" — separate the model's ability to *request* an action from the system's authority to *carry it out*. Why this is a seam: [[ToolHallucination]]'s closed-world rung asks *does the tool exist* — here the tool **exists and is invoked correctly**, so that rung passes by construction; [[HumanInTheLoop]] grades by impact × reversibility but **assumes the gate is synchronous and blocking**, which is precisely what the orchestrator broke; [[AgentSandbox]] bounds *what an action can touch*, not *who treated the call as already authorised* — the wiki's own "**authority is not isolation**" line now has a second, upstream member. It also finally gives [[AgentHarness]] a fifth thing the harness does that nobody had listed: **the harness is itself a trust principal** (it interprets text as an invocation), not a transparent pipe. ⚠️ Grade: **preprint, not yet peer-reviewed** (FSE 2027 acceptance is a venue, not a review); full transcript published **only for LangGraph**, the other five are "reproduced" with no detail; dummy tools (`make_bomb` / `launch_missiles`) with no real effect; servers tested at fixed code versions; **no claim of in-the-wild exploitation**; the 253 bugs are *verified* but their severity in any given deployment is not established. **Not ingested.**

**(2) NEW ⭐ 协议枢转 —— 跨智能体的授权继承 / Protocol Pivoting & Cross-Agent Authorization Inheritance (ledger row 10-16B).** Independent researcher **Syed Anas Mohiuddin** spent ~five months getting one class of MCP trust-boundary flaw confirmed and fixed at organisations with **nothing in common except the protocol**: **Google, JPMorgan Chase, Weaviate, France's DINUM** (data.gouv.fr MCP), **Indonesia's Tangerang city government** — plus five MCP servers for **US federal agencies** (incl. a VA-benefits one) reported 2026-09-02 and **still in triage** at publication. Reported by **Ars Technica** (Dan Goodin, **2026-10-05**). Mechanism: attacker plants text in content one agent reads; that agent passes it on **as a normal delegated task** to a peer it trusts; the peer sits inside the network and performs **SSRF** or another privileged action. "**Since MCP servers store credentials for each agent — and agents are built to trust every other internal agent — an exploit that would have been rejected by the LLM succeeds.**" The escalation is where the name comes from: the malicious text enters via **MCP** and crosses into **A2A** or the emerging **Agent Network Protocol**; "**trust or authorization gets effectively lost in translation**". Concrete: **Google's MCP Toolbox for Databases** (`googleapis/mcp-toolbox`) initialised its Go HTTP client **with no `CheckRedirect` policy and no target-IP validation** ⇒ a crafted path parameter follows a redirect to an internal endpoint, potentially the cloud metadata address (**169.254.169.254**, which hands out IAM credentials) — **CVE-2026-14540, CVSS 8.0**; fixed with resolved-address checks at connection time plus IP allow/block lists, and by **rejecting an unsafe base URL at startup rather than on first request** (Syed: "*That is what a real SSRF guard looks like. It is also more work than most MCP servers have done.*"). **Rapid7**'s related issue is **CVE-2026-97228, CVSS 2.7**, fixed in September. The root-cause sentence is Rapid7's Douglas McKee: "**Each protocol was built assuming it lived on its own, so each one checks its own front door while nobody watches the hallway in between.**" — and, operationally, "**dependency scanners do not see tool arguments that arrive as delegated agent text**". **Counter-evidence must sit beside it**: X41 D-Sec's **Markus Vervier** says the better term remains **indirect prompt injection** and that the cross-protocol hop "**is not strictly required for such attacks to work**". Why this is a seam: [[AgentIdentity]] attenuates the **credential** per hop (RFC 8693, strictly narrower scope/magnitude/validity/depth) but says nothing about the **task content** inheriting authority — the fix rule here is "**each protocol transition should trigger fresh authorization rather than inherit an upstream decision**", i.e. attenuation covers *who may act*, not *what was approved*. It is also the **first CVE-grade, cross-vendor evidence for [[MultiAgent]]'s cross-contamination**, which since 2026-09-26 has been recorded as a failure surface with **no evidence at all** (the whole page is flagged secondhand). Boundary against the existing 10-13「检索准入 × 跨主体状态隔离」row: that row asks *whose state is this* (leak inside **already-admitted shared state**); this row asks *on what authority did this instruction travel* (leak **at the hand-off**). ⚠️ Grade: pattern-level, largely the researcher's own framing amplified by Ars; **CVE-2026-14540** is attributed to his write-up (Ars reports severity 8 without naming the CVE); advisories **not re-opened first-hand** here; federal findings **unpatched / class-level only**; every named bug is **already fixed** — what is new is the cross-vendor pattern, not a zero-day. Syed presents at **MCPCon North America, 2026-10-23**. **Not ingested.**

**(3) NEW 厂商托管的记忆层 —— 第四个决定者 / Vendor-Operated Memory: The Fourth Decider (ledger row 10-16C; not ⭐).** **Meta Muse Wonder / Muse Wonder Full** (Meta Superintelligence Labs, **2026-10-12**, 1,048,576-token context, `$4 / $20` per Mtok, Muse Wonder Full **excluded from the API** — Muse Code subscription only) ships **Active Memory** as a model behaviour: it "carries important context about the user **across conversations** and retrieves the details relevant to the task at hand", with **MemoryBench** (Meta-internal: recall across 64K–100K-token conversations containing distractor conversations and injected turns that conflict with or later update the target details, LLM-judged) at **85.6 / 85.5** vs Claude Opus 5.5 **81.5**. Why this is a seam: [[LongTermMemory]] and [[MemoryGovernance]] were both written assuming **the store is one you build and operate** — [[MemoryGovernance]] explicitly enumerates **three** deciders (human-written rules / trained policy / **attacker**) and states that a vacant slot does not stay vacant. A memory layer that lives **inside a model API you do not operate** is a **fourth decider the page does not list**, and it moves three of the page's four bottom lines outside the deployer's reach: **who sets the retention period, what a deletion request actually removes, and which remembered detail influenced this output** (a provenance question [[Provenance]] has never been asked about memory). The reviewer's own framing of what is missing is the sharpest available statement of it: Meta's announcement describes no memory limits and no way to **review or clear what was stored**. Second, transferable half — **the model card's own cyber numbers disclose their own verification gap**: CyberGym **88.4%**, ExploitBench **58.9%**, ExploitGym **92 captured flags** — then a verification pass (a GPT-cyber verifier, independently confirmed with **Gemini 3.1 Pro**) found **only 21 came via the intended vulnerability and 71 via unintended ones**; since the published ranking counts intended-vulnerability exploitations only, **21 is the comparable figure**. A vendor publishing the number that survives path verification *inside its own model card* is the cleanest single teaching artifact this wiki has yet met for the standing "vendor self-report ≠ independently reproduced" discipline, and it generalises: **verify the path, not the headline count**. ⚠️ Grade: model card + announcement read via **secondary write-ups** (ai-tldr / traictory / digitbin / miraflow / dev.to), **not opened first-hand**; MemoryBench and FocusBench are **Meta-internal**; all safety numbers are for **Wonder Full**, which API users cannot run; the card itself warns performance varies with **prompts, tools and the agent harness**; pricing/availability claims partly via TechCrunch. **Not ingested.**

Ledger recomputed (56 → **59** rows; **all existing rows +1 day**; summary **14 ✅ / 5 🟡 / 40 ⏸**; **3 new rows**). 🎯 **unchanged** at **10-09「常驻上下文与指令预算」(⭐, 7 天)** — all three new rows are day 0 and therefore do not enter the criterion; the material-side oldest `⏸` is **09-17 RAG at 29 days**. Details in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-10-16.md`.

External signal note (for the record, not ingested): **today is the first radar day whose strongest evidence is a change in the *form* of the thing the wiki writes rules about, rather than a new participant or a new seam between existing parts.** Yesterday's signals were about *boundaries between participants* (model↔orchestrator, protocol↔protocol, deployer↔vendor). Today's two ⭐ candidates both concern **inspectability**: one replaces the *carrier* of memory with something unreadable, the other shows the *metric* used to judge memory cannot see what broke. Three recent Context/Compaction-adjacent pages ([[Provenance]], [[CitationLock]], [[Abstention]]) plus all four of [[MemoryGovernance]]'s bottom lines silently presuppose that whatever memory returns is **enumerable text**; today supplies the first concrete class of system for which that presupposition is false, and, separately, the first instrument that can detect memory-operation errors that answer-level metrics cannot see.

**(1) NEW ⭐ 记忆的第三种载体 —— 连续记忆 / Latent & Soft-Token Memory (ledger row 10-17A).** Every memory primitive in this wiki assumes what comes back is **text you can read**: [[Provenance]] attaches origin to a record, [[CitationLock]] permits citing only what was opened, [[Abstention]] refuses when nothing was opened, and [[MemoryGovernance]]'s four bottom lines — auditable · rollbackable · permissioned · signature-verified — are all stated over records that can **be enumerated**. **Memorilla** (`arXiv:2610.21375`, submitted **2026-10-15**, Fallahpour / Idehpour / Kothapalli / Mounier / Wu / Pardis / **Jure Leskovec**; Stanford · Toronto · UPenn · MIT · Vector · Arc; project site `memory.stanford.edu`) breaks that premise without losing quality: a frozen encoder turns each document into a reusable embedding, learned memory slots read the **whole collection plus the question**, and emit a fixed budget of **16 memory tokens** placed directly in the frozen LLM's input embedding space. Results (all author-reported, single frozen `Qwen3-8B` unless noted): best mean score (49.68) across eight benchmarks and leading on **five**, against Mem0 and MemoRAG; **394× fewer document tokens than full context on average** (>4,000× on NarrativeQA); on FactKG **84.33%** accuracy **vs 80.43% for full-context inference** — *beating* the un-compressed baseline while using **108×** fewer tokens; measured serving cost at 512 documents **0.74 s vs 19.9 s** (one H100-80GB, batch 1). In TextWorld it reduces wandering into already-explored rooms and lifts success **18.6% → 24.2%**. Second member, fitted to exactly [[Compaction]]'s failure mode: **REMORY** (`arXiv:2610.11287`, **2026-10-08**, Xia / Chen / Ge / Deng / Yang / Dong / Yuan / Zhu) trains a small external network that appends a **bounded run of continuous soft tokens after the textual summary** — "an analogue of a residual connection along the sequence dimension" — so a frozen LLM approximates the continuation it would have produced with the full history; on **SummHay** it approaches the full-context joint score using **5.2%** of input positions and raises **source attribution at nearly unchanged insight coverage**, and across long-horizon agent benchmarks **`Qwen3.8-27B` and `GLM-5.3-Flash` show consistent gains with substantially fewer repeated tool outputs and tool errors on BrowseComp and Terminal-Bench 2.1** (memory checkpoints released at 1.9B / 1.24B). Third member of the family, same week: **Cortex** (`arXiv:2610.20399`) distils context gains into **mergeable low-rank or FFN updates**, retaining **88.9%** of the context gain and **99.8%** of non-target performance after **50** sequential edits on a 14B model. Together they are three instances of one sentence already circulating in the literature: the three ways out of a growing context are **delete it · index it · compile it into weights** — and the third is the one with no page. Sharpest boundary statement for the wiki: **you cannot cite a vector.** A signed row can be verified; a learned memory token has **no address**, so there is nothing to attach a provenance record to, nothing to selectively delete, nothing for a human gate to review, and [[CitationLock]] cannot even be formulated for it. It also makes [[ContextEngineering]]'s "minimal high-signal **token** set" literal in a new way: the signal that reaches the model need not be tokens at all. Boundary: it is a **carrier**, not a fourth cluster — it answers the same question as 10-13's merged master row (*what does context/what survives rest on?*), but that row's two carriers (history vs task object) are **both text**, so it does not cover this one. Counter-evidence and open test recorded for [[MemoryGovernance]]: none of these benchmarks track **fact evolution** — which is precisely what candidate (2) below measures, and what a compressed continuous store may be the worst at. Suggested next step (not executed): revise `concepts/LongTermMemory.md` / `concepts/MemoryGovernance.md` with a bounded section, rather than a new concept page.

**(2) NEW ⭐ 记忆评测的内禀化 —— 「回答对了」不等于「记住对了」/ Intrinsic vs Extrinsic Evaluation of Agent Memory (FEB-30) (ledger row 10-17B).** Cluster F rests on one unexamined instrument: whether the agent's **answers** look right is used as a proxy for whether its **memory** got it right. **FEB-30** (`arXiv:2610.22209`, submitted **2026-10-15**; Sebastian-Antonio Toma / Jean de Kernier / Mike Ply / Oliviu Matei; 14 pages; code and data released at `github.com/amazon-science/feb-30-benchmark`) names and measures the flaw: the paradigm "**conflates memory correctness with retrieval and generation quality**", producing **false positives where a correct response masks broken memory** and **false negatives where an incorrect response blames correct memory for a retrieval failure**. Construction is instructive on its own — **paradigm inversion**: documents are *generated from known fact trajectories*, so ground truth is deterministic and needs **no human annotation**; each of **30 fact-evolution trajectories** (6 diagnostic categories × 3 difficulty levels) says how a fact *should* evolve across multi-author artifacts (documents, emails, conversations), which allows **direct inspection of the memory store after each ingestion step**. Applying it to a representative memory system: **intrinsic and extrinsic evaluation diverge by an average of 16 percentage points**, with **44% of trajectories exceeding meaningful divergence thresholds**; and diagnostic decomposition finds the system **classifies memory operations (create · update · reinforce · supersede) correctly only 20% of the time** — a failure "**entirely invisible to extrinsic evaluation**", with failure modes differing systematically by category and difficulty. Why this is a seam rather than a new topic: the operation taxonomy is almost the same one [[MemoryGovernance]] already uses (its four measured actions are Capture / Update / Recall / QA, and the learned-policy branch puts **ADD · UPDATE · DELETE · NOOP** into the action space) — so today supplies, for the first time, an instrument that **scores those decisions directly** instead of inferring them from answer quality. It therefore bears on the wiki's own evidence hygiene at three points: [[DyadMem]]'s four-stage gold annotations and its headline *Gold-Memory strong / Full-Pipeline collapses* finding are both measured downstream; `overview.md`'s standing open question that **Cluster F's entire evidence base is author-reported and needs independent reproduction** gets a sharper form — reproduce not only the numbers but **whether the metric can see the failure at all**; and it ports the existing 10-08 thread "**评测的评测**" (`arXiv:2610.07274`: only 22.6% of recorded passes clear four checks) from agent *scores* to **memory operations**, the same move one layer down. Suggested next step (not executed): pair with row 10-17A — **apply an intrinsic operation-level instrument to a latent/soft-token memory**; no paper has done this, and it is exactly where a compressed continuous store should be expected to fail ([[Abstention]] has nothing to abstain *from* when there is no address).

**(3) NEW 以协调基质为中心 —— 可引用消息取代直接通信 / Substrate-Centric Coordination (ScaleMAS) (ledger row 10-17C; not ⭐).** Yesterday's `MultiAgent.md` revision supplied CVE-grade evidence **that** cross-contamination happens but no candidate **shape** that prevents it. **ScaleMAS** (`arXiv:2610.16120`, submitted **2026-10-12**; Zhenting Qi (Harvard) with Zhuokai Zhao, Rulin Shao, Kerem Oktar, Zhiyuan Yao, Himabindu Lakkaraju, Wen-tau Yih, Ansong Ni, Bhargavi Paranjape, Yilun Du — **FAIR at Meta + Harvard**) names three problems that grow with group size and diagnoses all three as artifacts of **agent-centric** design: **reading burden** grows with the group (message-passing delivers every sender's output; coordinator systems route every result into one context), **useful information is diluted** by shared work of unknown quality, and **failure becomes routine** (a lost agent can crash the whole run). Its note on the intermediate generation of designs is the useful part for this wiki: recent **blackboard-style** methods move coordination into a shared medium and thereby relieve **only the third** problem. ScaleMAS makes the medium *addressable and citable* instead of merely shared: asynchronous agents operate on a shared datastore of linked messages through three operations — **publish** (with optional *references to earlier messages it builds on*), **search** (top-k, ranked by relevance **and by how often the message is cited**, which the authors find to be an effective proxy for quality), and **visit** (by address). Because search returns at most k messages, **an agent's read stays bounded at any group size**; because messages persist after their producers leave, the group survives agent loss. Results across four agentic tasks: outperforms agent-centric **and** blackboard baselines **at lower inference compute**, continues to scale to **128 agents**, and tolerates losing agents midway (contrast: coordinator-based **AOrchestra loses more than half its score at only 20% dropout**). Why this closes yesterday's seam: [[MultiAgent]]'s shared workspace fails because peer output arrives **as context**, already indistinguishable in authority from the agent's own; ScaleMAS's answer is that peers should exchange **references they must be searched for**, and — the line worth keeping — **citation count becomes the ranking signal**, i.e. [[Provenance]] stops being a post-hoc audit trail and becomes the **coordination mechanism itself**, which is exactly what `overview.md` anticipated when it extended Provenance's object from *facts* to *the verification status of what you are treating as fact*. Author-stated limits recorded: inference-only (agents are not trained to use the substrate); 128 is a **compute constraint**, not an architectural ceiling; **prompt sensitivity** — the scheme depends on agents writing clear rationales and sub-task descriptions to be searchable. Boundary: it is the **fix shape** for a section written yesterday, not a new axis; [[Abstention]] also gets a new reading — a bounded read is a *forced* abstention from everything outside top-k, which is a design choice rather than a judgement.

Strengthenings today (existing rows, remarks only, no new lines besides the three above):
- **10-13「上下文的两种载体 × 四条路」merged master row** — its method thesis (periodic context reset escapes the idea basin) is corroborated a second time by an independent group: **Scaling Discovery Requires Forgetting** (UC Berkeley, `arXiv:2610.20563`) reports the plateau survives even when you simply add more agents — "*agents with scored verifiers plateau whether you scale one agent longer, run agents in parallel, or let them share candidates… each agent anchors on its own past attempts and makes smaller edits*"; periodically **deleting every session and the candidate history and restarting from the best candidate so far** gives up to **+15%** over parallel agents and **+39%** over a single agent at equal budget, on heuristic programming / kernel optimization / model-training tasks. ⚠️ Evidence grade: this paper was read only through a secondary aggregator digest; the arXiv page was not opened first-hand, so it is recorded as direction-and-magnitude only. Note the **tension** it introduces with the self-evolving-agent thread (Harness² / Evo² improve *by keeping* edit history; this argues history is what stalls discovery) — possibly reconcilable (*keep how edits were made, discard what was tried*), but untested on one task; recorded because it is this row's first **counter-result from a different lab**, not merely a replication.
- **10-15C / harness-measurement thread** — **Doc2Harness** (`arXiv:2610.20344`, HF daily #4) is the instrument that row lacked: it benchmarks **building a harness**, not running one; best model (`GPT-6 Astra`) passes **53.05%** of **328** tests, worst **27.44%**, and failures cluster where **cross-cutting features span modules**. Per this wiki's standing two-column rule this is a *measurement of construction*, still author-reported, and it quantifies what [[AgentHarness]] has asserted qualitatively since 2026-09-26.

Boundaries vs [[Compaction]] (REMORY is residual compensation for the summary — a second leg, not a replacement) · [[ContextRot]] (two exits on one axis) · [[ContextEngineering]] ("minimum high-signal **token** set" rewritten literally — the signal need not be tokens). Counter-evidence kept on screen: **all figures author-reported**, Memorilla's mainline is a **single frozen `Qwen3-8B`**, and **none of these benchmarks track fact evolution**.
- **`concepts/MemoryGovernance.md`** — two additions. **(a) A scope note on the four governance bottom lines:** all four are written against an **enumerable** bank; remove enumerability and **three of the four become unstatable** (only *signature-verified* survives, because signatures attach to the write, not the entry) ⇒ a new prior question to ask *before* the four: **is the thing being governed addressable at all, and if not, what replaces the audit?** (candidate replacement: govern the **write event** plus a pre/post behavioural diff). **(b) A new section「"Right answer" ≠ "right memory" — intrinsic vs extrinsic evaluation」** for **FEB-30** (`arXiv:2610.22209`, 2026-10-15; code `github.com/amazon-science/feb-30-benchmark`): the paradigm inversion (documents generated **backwards from known fact trajectories** ⇒ deterministic ground truth, no annotation; 30 trajectories × 6 diagnostics × 3 difficulties, store body inspectable **after every ingestion**), the results (**16 pp** average divergence, **44%** over threshold, only **20%** of create/update/reinforce/**supersede** judged correct and **invisible to extrinsic evaluation**), and why it is a seam on this page (FEB-30's taxonomy is near-isomorphic to Capture/Update/Recall/QA and to ADD·UPDATE·DELETE·NOOP ⇒ **first instrument scoring the operation layer directly**; it also sharpens the Cluster F open question into「reproduce whether the ruler can see the failure, before reproducing the numbers」). Limits on screen: single "representative" system, 30 trajectories, **no independent replication**.
- **The merged open experiment** (subsection of (b), cross-linked from [[LongTermMemory]]): point FEB-30's instrument at a **continuous / latent** memory. **No paper has done this**; on first principles it is where compressed continuous storage breaks, because with no enumerable entries **there is no interface for "which older fact is being superseded"** — and `supersede` is already correct only 20% of the time in *addressable* stores. Stated as a **hypothesis, not a result**: extrinsic scores stay competitive while intrinsic correctness collapses ⇒ the divergence would be **a property of the carrier**, not of the system under test. Minimum viable form recorded: one trajectory set × two stores (addressable baseline vs soft-token) at matched budgets, scored both ways after each ingestion, **report the divergence, not the leaderboard**.

Four points as recommended: ① the three problems that grow with group size (**read burden** · **useful signal diluted by unknown-quality shared work** · **failure becomes normal**), diagnosed as a product of **agent-centric** design; ② the key judgement that **blackboard-style shared layers solve only the third**; ③ three operations over a **linked message store** (**publish** with citations · **search** top-*k* ranked by **relevance × citation count** · **fetch by address**) ⇒ bounded read volume at any group size + persistence after the producer leaves; ④ results — beats agent-centric and blackboard baselines on four agentic tasks **at lower inference compute**, scales to **128 agents**, and the number to remember: orchestrator-style **AOrchestra loses more than half its score at only 20% drop-out**. Two wiki-level repairs are recorded with it: **[[Provenance]] promoted from after-the-fact audit to in-flight coordination** (citation count as a ranking signal = the runnable form of overview's 2026-09-26 prediction), and **a second form of [[Abstention]]** — top-*k* bounded reading as **architectural forced abstention**, distinct from abstention after judgement. Author-stated limits on screen: inference-time only, **128 is a compute ceiling not an architectural one**, **prompt-sensitive**.

External signal note (for the record, not ingested): **today is the first radar day whose three candidates all land on the same grammatical object — a verb, not a noun.** The prior twelve scanning lines each added a variable to a mechanism that already existed (where it sits, whose state it is, what it costs, how long it lives, whether anyone has written a spec for it, whether anyone checks the stretch between two parts, whether the governed object is even readable). Today's three share one previously unasked question: **has anyone measured whether the verb itself succeeds, separately from whether the rule governing it is well-designed?** This wiki is unusually well-stocked with rules about operations and correspondingly thin on instruments that test the operations.

**(1) NEW ⭐ Does deletion actually work? — the `DELETE` half has never been measured (ledger row 10-18A).** Two independent benchmarks landed in the same week. **Bemula** (`arXiv:2610.18052`, submitted **2026-10-13**; 50 multi-session dialogue instances / 746 sessions / **135** forget requests across six types / **1,742** test questions) is described by its authors as the first benchmark dedicated to memory unlearning. Its design decision is the load-bearing one: **every dialogue is paired with a control version that never received the forget request**, so only a system that demonstrably stored the target can earn credit for forgetting it — which closes the degenerate escape routes (never store it, wipe everything, refuse to answer related questions) that would otherwise score perfectly. Result, stated in the authors' own words: "**no system passes more than 1.5% of forget requests end to end, and most pass none at all**". Failures run in both directions: the target stays extractable under simple attacks, **or** the system deletes far more than requested. **MemCut** (`arXiv:2610.21405`, submitted **2026-10-15**; 456 hand-designed secrets with fully annotated evidence trails) supplies the second instrument, splitting **native recovery** (can the system's own retrieval surface the secret?) from **residual recovery** (can the same reader model reconstruct the secret when handed the surviving fragments?). Removing the explicit disclosure reduces native recovery far more than residual recovery; after LLM-guided deletion of ten further turns from twenty retrieval candidates, **39–76% residual recovery** remains; and subset sweeps (144 facts / three systems / two models) show that **widening the candidate pool at a fixed budget reduces residual recovery more than deleting all the original candidates** ⇒ the bottleneck is **candidate coverage**, not deletion strength. Why this is the seam rather than a new topic: [[MemoryGovernance]] puts **DELETE** into the learned policy's action space (ADD · UPDATE · DELETE · NOOP) and lists **rollbackable** as one of its four bottom lines, while its new 2026-10-17 scope note begins "all four are written against an **enumerable** bank" — today supplies the complementary negative: even where the bank *is* enumerable and every address exists, the delete does not reliably take effect. It also finally gives both halves of `overview.md`'s recorded 2026-10-09 corollary ("**'failed to forget' is a deductible score (FAMA) — and so is 'deleted something still in use'**") separate measurement instruments. Boundary worth recording explicitly: the ledger's **09-24「Memory Lifecycle Governance / Agent Forgetting」row is long since ✅**, but it answers *when* to forget and *who decides*; today answers *whether the deletion took* — policy vs. efficacy, not a repeat. Author-stated limits carried alongside: both are single preprints, first-of-their-kind benchmarks, no independent replication; MemCut explicitly scopes to **text** memory and excludes parametric / multimodal stores (which is why it does not yet bear on 10-17's latent-carrier section). Suggested next step (not executed): a bounded efficacy note on `concepts/MemoryGovernance.md` at the four bottom lines.

**(2) NEW ⭐ Memory formation poisoning — five interfaces, and no artifact to inspect (ledger row 10-18B).** **MemForm** (`arXiv:2610.14431`, cs.CR; code `github.com/opanhw/memform-code`) reframes memory poisoning away from attack channels / corruption patterns / lifecycle stages and onto **the interfaces through which persistent memory is produced and committed**: **Content · Evidence · Scope · Selection · Path**, the last two introduced by this paper to isolate **retention** and **consolidation-order** effects. Across six LLMs and two response formats, all five interfaces show broad susceptibility; evaluated defenses mitigate incompletely and unevenly. The sentence that carries the row: "**authentic experiences with successful outcomes do not guarantee reliable behavior after memory formation**" — and specifically, "**consolidation order can alter behavior even with identical retained experiences**". This removes the precondition every existing wiki defense silently relies on: there is no poisoned artifact to scan. Every record can be authentic, correctly sourced, properly retrieved, and [[Provenance]] / [[CitationLock]] / [[Abstention]] will each report a clean result — because the manipulation is in the *procedure*, not the content. Boundary: [[MemoryPoisoning]]'s two recorded mechanisms (PMPA induced self-write · Darktrace forged conversation history) are both **content** attacks; this row is the third shape, one layer earlier, and is why "content is clean" must stop being read as "the store is safe". It rhymes with the 2026-10-16 ToFu line (order is a property of the framework's implementation) and the 2026-10-17 FEB-30 line (`supersede` judged correct only 20% of the time), but located in the **memory consolidation pipeline** rather than the orchestration or evaluation pipeline. Suggested next step (not executed): extend `concepts/MemoryPoisoning.md` from two mechanisms to five interfaces.

**(3) NEW Context-engineering techniques are conditional, not universally good (ledger row 10-18C; not ⭐, but it closes a standing overview open question).** **Same Context, Different Agents** (`arXiv:2610.20959`, submitted **2026-10-14**; USC + **Oracle AI**, incl. **Dan Roth**) runs three context-engineering techniques — semantic layers, orchestration, compaction — alone and combined, across **seven** LLMs, **five** benchmarks and **1,514** long-horizon tasks. Without any CET, data agents split into three behaviour groups: **investigators**, **premature answerers** (answer without inspecting the data), and **early stoppers** (give up). The ruling result: **each CET delivers its known mechanistic benefit, but only the behaviour group it repairs converts that benefit into accuracy.** For investigators the same three CETs are actively harmful — the semantic layer stops them looking, orchestration makes them overconfident (**−5 percentage points**), compaction makes them repeat steps. Magnitudes worth keeping: orchestration rescues premature answerers for up to **+23 accuracy points at 4× cost**; semantic layers rescue early stoppers for **+4.6 points at near-zero extra cost**; **combining CETs yields smaller gains and losses than the sum of the parts**; and the single most common error — selecting the wrong data slice after having looked — **is fixed by none of them**. This is the direct answer to `overview.md`'s open question of 2026-09-09 ("**how [[ContextEngineering]] techniques are measured end-to-end beyond single-provider claims — needs an independent evaluation**"), and it arrives in the counter-result form that [[MultiAgent]] established on 2026-09-26 (the verifier is the precondition). Portable rule for the wiki: **diagnose the failure mode before prescribing the technique** — a CET is a repair for a specific habit, not a quality upgrade. Limits on screen: single preprint limited to the data-agent domain; behaviour groups are derived from clustering twelve trace features, so cross-model transferability is the load-bearing assumption; cost figures are relative. Suggested next step (not executed): revise `concepts/ContextEngineering.md` with a "techniques are repairs, not upgrades" section.

Strengthenings today (existing rows, remarks only, no new lines besides the three above):
- **10-09E「承诺的可行性 / 空承诺」row** — today's row 1 is a silent corroboration from a different direction: that benchmark's judgment criterion is that infeasibility is "**a property of the configuration, not of the trajectory**" (judgeable within a single turn). Bemula's paired-control design applies the same instinct to *deletion*: credit for forgetting is a property of the store's state after the request, not of how agreeable the answer sounded. Two rows, one method.
- **10-15B「写入侧的弃权 / Write-Side Abstention」row** — MemForm's **Selection** interface (retention) is the adversarial twin of that row's entry-side threshold: where 10-15B shows a single confidence gate cannot separate categories on the way *in*, MemForm shows retention effects survive even when every admitted item is legitimate.
- **[[Compaction]]** gains its strongest side-effect number to date from a paper recorded as 备查 rather than a row: **Prune the Summary, Not the History** (`arXiv:2610.20927`) reports that **83% of failed runs using a widely used structured compressor trace back to an action carrying out a specific summary entry that no environment output supports** — compaction summaries mix environment-established facts with the compressor's own written-in reasoning, and the agent cannot tell them apart afterwards. Its Source-Judged Compaction post-processing step lifts cross-run reliability **+2.1%–8.9%** on AppWorld / OfficeBench at unchanged token usage. Not registered as a row because it is second-instance evidence for a page this wiki already covers; recorded here so the number is findable.

**Thirteenth scanning line (method increment) — 动词实效审计 / verb-efficacy audit.** Ask of every mechanism already written down: *this mechanism depends on a verb succeeding — a deletion taking effect, a consolidation committing in a given order, a technique being applied, a history being compressed. Has anyone measured whether that verb itself succeeds, separately from whether the rule that governs it is good?* It bites [[MemoryGovernance]] (DELETE sits in the learned-policy action space and one of four bottom lines — **rollbackable** — rests entirely on it, yet no instrument existed until this week), [[MemoryPoisoning]] (assumes a detectable poisoned artifact is what you are looking for), [[ContextEngineering]] (assumes its techniques are upgrades rather than repairs), [[Compaction]] (83% of failures traceable to unsupported summary entries), and [[Provenance]] / [[CitationLock]] / [[Abstention]] (all three can return clean verdicts on every record while the procedure that assembled them was manipulated). Relationship to the twelve prior lines: they asked *where it sits, whose state it is, what it costs, how long it lives, whether it survives another principal, whether anyone wrote a spec, whether anyone checks the seam, whether it is readable*; line twelve asked **"can you see the thing?"**, and line thirteen asks the immediate next question — **"even if you can see it, did what you just did to it take?"**

External signal note (for the record, not ingested): **today is the first radar day whose three candidates are all instances of one variable swap — the container, not the content.** The prior thirteen scanning lines each added a variable to a mechanism that already existed (where it sits, whose state it is, what it costs, how long it lives, whether anyone has written a spec for it, whether anyone checks the stretch between two parts, whether the governed object is even readable, whether the verb took). Today's three share one previously unasked question: **hold the payload fixed and change only how it arrives — does the conclusion survive?**

**(1) NEW ⭐ The framing gap — the wrapper, not the payload, decides whether an injection works (ledger row 10-19A).** **TRACER** (`arXiv:2610.19133`, submitted **2026-10-13**; Kshitij Mishra / Salem Lahlou / **Nils Lukas**, MBZUAI; **accepted to Findings of EMNLP 2026** — the first radar row this month carrying a peer-reviewed venue rather than a preprint) holds the attack payload fixed and rewraps it. Across the authors' four presentation channels: **follow-up message 70% → agent message 92% → tool output 95% → retrieved context 96%** (the authors' "authority gradient" reading of their own panel). The instrument-level number: six checkpoints trained only against follow-up attacks score **18.0%** ASR under follow-up evaluation and **53%** under RAG evaluation — **+35 percentage points from the wrapper alone**. Why this is the seam rather than a new topic: it lands on the exact channel this wiki's three honesty disciplines bless. [[Provenance]] says every fact carries origin / time / evidence; [[CitationLock]] says an answer may cite only evidence actually opened; [[Abstention]] says no sourced evidence ⇒ refuse. Together they make **"retrieved and opened" the gold standard of trustworthy content** — and TRACER measures that same channel as the most effective one at **96%**. The load-bearing sentence: **the most trustworthy-looking kind of evidence is also the most effective delivery vehicle.** It also reorganises two existing rows: [[ToolHallucination]]'s closed-world rung asks *does this tool exist* — TRACER shows a **real** tool returning that payload is a **95%** channel, so registry membership is not the variable; and 10-18B MemForm ("content is clean does not imply the store is safe") now has its counterpart — here the content is *not* clean, and what varies is only its clothing. Portable rule: **the authority gradient is a property of the wrapper, and the wrapper is the one thing no entry-side discipline inspects.** Limits on screen: GSM8K arithmetic with `Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct` as the defended model; the ASR is conditional (an attack counts only if the model answered correctly before the injection and incorrectly after); the 70/92/95/96 ordering is the authors' own interpretation of their own panel; TRACER's own defence still leaves **6.1%** RAG-framed ASR and **13.9%** average across framings at 3B (**9.4%** at 7B) — it narrows the gap, it does not close it. Suggested next step (not executed): a section on `concepts/Provenance.md` recording that provenance metadata makes a payload *more* effective, not less.

**(2) NEW ⭐ The repair gap — this wiki is almost entirely detectors, and acting on the detection is where it breaks (ledger row 10-19B).** Two instruments in one week. **CLASHarness** (`arXiv:2610.19128`, submitted **2026-10-13**; Yuxiang Lai et al.) generates verifiable multi-agent conflicts in computer use — **600** multi-round tasks, **15** frontier models, with decisive visual facts pre-committed before rendering and graders compiled from executed reference trajectories so every task is solvable by construction. Three bottlenecks, of which the second carries the row: **"resolving conflicts is much harder than finding them"** — agents can spot inconsistencies, but *actively fixing system state drops task success significantly*. Third: coordination protocols are a universal bottleneck — **even frontier models fail over 58% of closing actions**, typically forgetting to release shared locks or to hand off the task. **ARIA** (`arXiv:2610.20780`, submitted **2026-10-14**, announced 10-16; Bolin Shen / Yushun Dong; code `github.com/LabRAI/ARIA`) supplies the answering half: **24,000+** trajectories across four MAS frameworks, six benchmarks and eight host models, filtered into **3,953 execution-verified repair pairs** — a pair is retained only when the repaired execution passes and its unmodified counterpart fails. Its framing sentence: *"Diagnosing completed runs or repeatedly re-executing them to search for corrections does not provide a learned policy for intervening while execution is still in progress."* A **7B** ARIA model reports state-of-the-art executed repair accuracy in both runtime and post-hoc settings, outperforming zero-shot frontier models. Why this is a seam and not a fourth parallel topic: enumerate the wiki's mechanisms and they are nearly all **detectors** — [[ToolHallucination]]'s closed-world rung, [[Provenance]] / [[CitationLock]] / [[Abstention]], [[HumanInTheLoop]]'s grading, [[MultiAgent]]'s verifier, [[MemoryGovernance]]'s four actions, 10-16A ToFu, 10-18C's behaviour groups. **None of them has a written successor verb.** CLASHarness then makes the omission active rather than passive: the wiki's standing advice ("verify your peers' outputs before inheriting them") is, in that benchmark, the step whose execution **lowers** task success. Boundary: this is not a second instance of 10-18's verb-efficacy line — that line asked whether a verb that *exists* works; this row is about a verb that was **never written down**, and CLASHarness's 58% concerns protocol hygiene (locks / handoffs), a class this wiki has no page for at all. The 10-17 rule applies: **when a rule's precondition fails, record it as a boundary, not a refutation** — the detectors are not wrong, they are incomplete, and the incompleteness is the part that costs. Limits on screen: CLASHarness is a generated benchmark in the computer-use domain; ARIA's numbers are author-reported and its generalisation claims are to unseen frameworks / host models within the same training pipeline; "7B outperforms frontier" is a repair-specific claim, not a general-capability one. Suggested next step (not executed): a section on `concepts/MultiAgent.md` — "finding ≠ fixing" — carrying the 58% closing-action figure.

**(3) NEW The communication channel stops being text (ledger row 10-19C; not ⭐).** **Swarm Telepathy / SwaT** (`arXiv:2610.15095`; MIT CSAIL + MIT-IBM Watson AI Lab; Alabdulkareem / Tenison / Zhang / Zhu / Katz / Gutfreund) is described as the first **cross-family** KV-cache transfer method, allowing prefill-free communication in multi-family multi-agent systems: combined with KV-cache compaction it reports a mean **40%** higher accuracy and **4×** faster wall clock than text-to-text, across **17** benchmarks / **12** LLMs / **6** families, with sender and receiver both frozen (<50M training tokens, <1 GPU-day). **CacheFuse** (training-free, linear interpolation of the upstream KV cache into a downstream model) reports up to **+8.6 percentage points** and **12.2×** TTFT speedup over text-based inter-model communication. A third line, the UIUC DREAM Lab **Agent Primitives** work, reports the asymmetry from the other side: with an auxiliary instruction placed **in the middle** of a long context, natural-language communication retains it at **15.6%** while KV-cache communication retains **73.3%**; under injected noise (10 irrelevant sentences) NL accuracy falls to **47%** vs KV **93%**. Why this is the seam: 10-17's load-bearing sentence was **"you cannot cite a vector"**, written about the **memory store**. Same argument, new location — and the new location is precisely the apparatus this wiki installed two days ago. 10-17C ScaleMAS's three operations are **publish with citations · search top-*k* ranked by relevance × citation count · fetch by address**; every one of them presupposes the message is **text**. Let the channel become a KV cache and that apparatus has no object — and [[Provenance]], promoted on 10-17 from after-the-fact audit to **in-flight coordination**, is promoted onto a medium that cannot carry a citation. Relationship to the prior lines: 10-12 asked "the model is not the only place to put a control", 10-17 asked "is the governed object readable"; this row asks the same question of the **wire** rather than the store. It also cuts the other way on the numbers: if KV communication is the more faithful channel, the text channel's lossiness was doing unnoticed work. Limits on screen: SwaT's gains are measured against **text-to-text** communication, not against a strong text baseline with good summarisation; cross-family transfer is trained on text-only data and the vision-transfer result is a demonstration; the instruction-retention figures come from a separate group with its own setup; and **none of these papers addresses provenance at all** — the wiki's inference that citations become unstatable is **this wiki's own extrapolation, not a measured result**, and must be written as a boundary, not as a finding.

Strengthenings today (existing rows, remarks only, no new lines besides the three above):
- **10-16A ToFu「顺序是框架实现的属性」** — TRACER is the same shape one layer over: where ToFu says the *sequence* is a property of the framework's implementation, TRACER says the *success rate* is a property of the presentation wrapper. Both are today's container variable; ToFu can now be cited as its earliest instance.
- **10-18C「上下文工程技法的条件性」** — also a container swap (same technique, different behaviour group), retroactively its second instance.
- **10-17B FEB-30 / 10-18A Bemula** — neither is a container swap; they belong to 10-18's verb-efficacy line. Recorded so the two lines do not get merged later.

**Fourteenth scanning line (method increment) — 容器审计 / container audit.** Ask of every conclusion already written down: *hold the payload fixed and change only the wrapper it arrives in, or the step it occupies — does the conclusion survive?* It bites [[Provenance]] / [[CitationLock]] (the retrieved-and-opened channel is the 96% one), [[ToolHallucination]] (a *real* tool returning the payload is a 95% channel), [[MultiAgent]] (detection is written down, repair is not; 58% of closing actions fail), and [[Provenance]] a second time via 10-17C (its citation apparatus presupposes a text wire). **Today's radar (30th run) — three candidates, two ⭐, all registered in the ledger (68 → 71 rows; summary 14 ✅ / 10 🟡 / 47 ⏸).** Full write-up in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-10-20.md`; the load-bearing numbers are recorded here so `log.md` stays the durable copy:
- **10-20A ⭐ — Scoped vs unscoped revision in latent memory（ScopeMem, `arXiv:2610.26503`, submitted 2026-10-19 14:38 UTC; Zhicheng Zheng / Cheng Zhang / Shengping Yu / Tianxiang Zhao; 13pp / 5 fig / 9 tab).** Latent memory supplies history to a **frozen** VLM via prefix vectors trained with step-wise CE. Gains are "naturally attributed to encoded history", but **controlled experiments show they primarily stem from action revision** — the prefix learns to alter the model's default actions. That revision is **unscoped**: step-wise CE pushes outputs toward reference actions **at every step** without explicitly preserving correct decisions ⇒ **it repairs errors but also breaks 8–13% of initially correct answers**, and "*overall accuracy metrics hide these losses*". ScopeMem conditions prefix generation on **the model's initial answer** and is trained to correct errors **while preserving correct decisions** ⇒ broken-correct share down to **5.6%** on the controlled ablation set, while repairing more errors than any evaluated CE-trained prefix; on AndroidControl / GUI-Odyssey / zero-shot AITW it beats all evaluated prompting and latent-memory baselines, letting a frozen `Qwen3-VL-8B` match or surpass GUI-specialised agents and `Qwen3-VL-32B`. **Why it is a seam:** it names the verb latent memory actually performs — **revision**, i.e. [[MemoryGovernance]]'s UPDATE/supersede executed **on a non-addressable carrier** (the very operation FEB-30 finds correct only 20% of the time in *addressable* stores) — and it is **the same shape as 10-17B FEB-30** (extrinsic evaluation hides operation-layer failure / aggregate accuracy hides per-decision loss) ⇒ the 10-17 open experiment has its **first partially-aligned empirical foothold**. Counter-evidence: 5.6% is on the authors' own ablation set; 8–13% is specific to CE-trained prefixes; results are GUI-agent + single frozen 8B; no independent replication.
- **10-20B ⭐ — Owner attribution in agent memory: exposure ≠ behavior ≠ state（"Whose Credential Is It?", `arXiv:2610.25707`, submitted 2026-10-19 02:53 UTC; Hau-Sing So et al.; 19pp / 14 fig / 12 tab; AppWorld).** A **three-stage audit** separates a foreign credential reaching the prompt (**exposure**), the agent acting with it (**behavior**), and the other user's account changing (**state**). A store mixing **763** past logins from all users retrieves another user's credential for **84.7%** of required apps — **yet agents rarely act on it**. Misuse appears under a **stress test** (memory lacks the user's own credential and presents a single foreign one as the user's record): seven agents then use it in **80–94%** of runs and change the victim's account in up to **43%**, **sometimes passing the official check**. **Naming the owner before or inside each credential line** ⇒ misuse falls **92 → 1 of 305** paired runs (also with name-free usernames), at a cost of **3–5 successful tasks per model** on clean memory; **a wrongly written owner largely restores misuse**, and trailing tags / added safety instructions help only some models. **Why it is a seam:** the wiki measures *exposure* throughout ([[MemoryPoisoning]], [[MemoryGovernance]]'s Recall, the 10-13 admissibility row) — this shows exposure and behavior can differ by nearly an order of magnitude in both directions; and the fix **depends on per-line addressability**, so it has nowhere to live on continuous memory ⇒ a direct counterpoint to 10-17A. It is also the **instrument** for the variable 10-13 / 10-14A named (**ownership ≠ permission**). Control group recorded for the first time: **MemLeak** (`arXiv:2610.04195`) reports **70–100%** incidental cross-user leakage and **90–100%** adversarial top-*k* placement, with end-to-end contamination **5.00/5**, and finds that of three architectural mitigations **only hard post-retrieval ownership gating** restores the clean baseline (**1.00/5**, ≈**1.4 ms/query**) — i.e. MemLeak stops at exposure, this row walks two stages further. Limits: the stress test is **constructive, not an incidence estimate**; MemLeak self-declares **hand-written synthetic cases and small samples** (a mechanism, not a frequency).
- **10-20C — Persistence of directives & authority erosion（Structured Authority Injection / SAI, `arXiv:2610.22373v1`; listed in the **cs.AI 2026-10-19** new-submissions page; Ryan Tran et al.; not ⭐).** The stated control gap: **no protocol-level mechanism lets an operator inject binding directives that persist across planning cycles.** Free-text overrides fail twice — **ambiguous interpretation** (no schema validation) and **authority erosion** (operator instructions lose influence inside accumulating context). SAI routes corrections through **typed, schema-validated MCP tool calls** and maintains a **ranked authority context prepended to every prompt**, with three override classes carrying distinct persistence semantics: **Constraint** (hard ones never violated; soft ones bypassed only if no compliant assignment exists; **persist until explicitly lifted**) · **Priority** (urgency from external channels; **expires when the affected task or task class completes**) · **Strategy** (coordination heuristics; whole mission, or **exactly N planning cycles**); conflicts resolved **C > P > S** computed **at prompt-build time**. The load-bearing shape is the **asymmetry**: override tools are callable **only by the supervision interface — never by the planning agent**, which can **read** the orders but cannot create or delete them (⇒ the same "read right ≠ write right" family as [[MemoryGovernance]]'s *Permissioned* bottom line and 10-17C's citable messages). Nine LLMs × eight failure-mode scenarios: reduced ambiguity and mitigated authority decay, with prompt overhead **down up to 38%** in the strongest models (GPT-4o; DeepSeek-R1 31%; GPT-OSS-20B from zero to all tasks in two scenarios). **Costs on screen:** Gemini-2.0-Flash drops **1.00 → 0.50** in the zone-distribution scenario; DeepSeek-R1 **exhausts its 4,096-token budget** in S7 as the authority context grows; LLaMA / Qwen / Claude-Haiku gain cost **without proportional improvement** (the authors' own weakest group). **Why it matters here:** [[HumanInTheLoop]] is the wiki's only detect-then-act chain, and it has **no persistence dimension** — this is that page's missing half; and it is the **first citable mechanism the 🎯 10-09「常驻上下文与指令预算」row has had in three weeks**, since it turns "what deserves to be always-loaded" into *typed data with an explicit expiry*. ⚠️ **Date discrepancy recorded:** the `arxiv.org/abs/2610.22373` page metadata reads "Submitted on 27 Jul 2026", contradicting the `2610` ID and the cs.AI 2026-10-19 listing; this entry follows **the listing**, and the inconsistency is logged rather than silently resolved.

**Fifteenth scanning line (method increment) — 粒度审计 / granularity audit.** Ask of every number already written down: *hold the measured object fixed and change only the granularity at which you observe it (aggregate / per-item / per-stage / process) — does the number still hold?* Registered in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/SCAN-LINES.md` **before** being referenced anywhere, per that file's lock rule 2. It completes a three-way distinction with lines twelve and fourteen, which are otherwise easy to conflate: **twelve changes the 东西** (the governed thing itself ⇒ rules lose their conditions of statement) · **fourteen changes the 盒子** (the garment / the step ⇒ the number is not a property of the object) · **fifteen changes the 尺子的刻度** (the granularity ⇒ the number is a property of the ruler). **Retrospective re-filing:** **10-17B FEB-30** (intrinsic vs extrinsic, **16 pp** average divergence) is now recognised as the **earliest instance** of line fifteen, with today's three rows supplying further instances (ScopeMem's **8–13%**; exposure / behavior / state; and — recorded as 备查 — LHTB 2.0's binary **0.221** vs dense-ladder **0.653**). This is the **second** scanning line (after fourteen) able to re-classify existing rows.

**Standing for the record:** nothing was ingested ⇒ `raw/` untouched; `overview.md` and `concept-relationship.md` left unchanged (the structural suggestion carried by 10-19B — a repair-side inventory in `overview.md`'s Cross-Cutting Themes — is **recorded on `concepts/MultiAgent.md` but not executed**, per the ingest-only convention); **nothing written to `learning-materials/`** (README「人工核查承诺」).

**(1) What was added.** `#### Scope note — the three operations presuppose a text wire` — placed **inside** the existing 10-17C ScaleMAS subsection rather than as a sibling section, because it names that mechanism's *precondition*; the same anti-drift rationale that put 10-17C inside 10-16B's section. Content: **Swarm Telepathy / SwaT** (`arXiv:2610.15095`; MIT CSAIL + MIT-IBM Watson AI Lab; Alabdulkareem / Tenison / Zhang / Zhu / Katz / Gutfreund) — first **cross-family** KV-cache transfer, prefill-free, both sender and receiver **frozen** (<50M training tokens, <1 GPU-day); with KV-cache compaction a mean **40%** higher accuracy and **4×** faster wall clock **than text-to-text**, over **17** benchmarks / **12** LLMs / **6** families. **CacheFuse** (training-free linear interpolation of the upstream cache into the downstream model) up to **+8.6 pp** and **12.2×** TTFT.
**(2) The load-bearing move:** 10-17's sentence **"you cannot cite a vector"** was written about the **memory store**; 10-19C relocates it to the **wire**. **Today's radar (31st run) — three candidates, two ⭐, all registered in the ledger (71 → 74 rows; summary 14 ✅ / 10 🟡 / 50 ⏸).** Full write-up in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/2026-10-21.md`; the load-bearing numbers are recorded here so `log.md` stays the durable copy:
- **10-21A ⭐ — 记忆的保护一致性：标签检查替代内容审查（ClawMMU, `arXiv:2610.28093`, submitted 2026-10-20 12:09 UTC; Jinhao Hu / Bardia Mohammadi / Mofasshara Binte Rafique / Lars Henning Klein / Akhil Arora / Ashvin Goel / Laurent Bindschaedler; MPI-SWS · EPFL · Aarhus · Toronto; **AgenticOS Workshop @ NeurIPS 2026 (non-archival)**, 6pp / 1 fig / 1 tab).** 现有外壳**不记录「谁可以对这条记忆做什么」**，因此「抓取到的网页里藏的一条指令」会被当成用户自己的指令执行。ClawMMU 给每一份记忆的**每个表征**挂一个 **protection descriptor**，回答四问：**owner**（谁产生的）/ **scope**（哪些任务与会话可以用）/ **origins**（由哪些页面派生）/ **authority**（这段内容可以证成什么，有序层级：**Evidence** 最低=工具输出与网页检索「可读但永不可据此行动」→ 中间层=用户偏好 / 队友的指令文件 → **Policy** 最高=用户消息）。检查发生在**四次穿越上下文边界**之处：**map**（进入上下文时按来源派发描述符；挡 address-space violation）· **derive**（写入存储时按继承规则附上描述符；挡 type confusion——不受信文本经摘要变成 policy）· **reload**（从存储取回 / checkpoint 恢复时比对撤销计数器；挡 use-after-revoke）· **authorize**（工具调用时逐参数检查是否可追溯到已授权且未被撤销的内容；挡 confused deputy）。**承重规则：information may rise in authority, but authority never lifts itself** —— 继承规则取**输入中最低的权威**，且**模型自身被视为最低权威输入** ⇒ 模型自己写下的记忆**永远是 Evidence，只有 owner 能提升它**。⇒ 判定**只依赖内容来自哪里，不依赖它说了什么**，于是记忆攻击退化为**经典的保护错误**，**即使模型被骗也能被外壳拦住**。同时它点破了 [[HumanInTheLoop]] 一处从未写下的缺口：生产外壳在高危工具调用前确实会问人，**但这些检查不知道参数来自哪里** —— `authorize` 正是补的这一格。**Why it is a seam:** 昨日 10-20B 测出「逐行标出所有者」把滥用从 92/305 压到 1/305，本行交付的是**那台测量所对应的架构**；且 [[MemoryGovernance]] 的第三条底线 *Permissioned* 第一次有了可执行机制而非原则。**必须同屏的反面证据（本行最重要的一条）：🔴 ClawMMU 尚未实现 —— 附录 C 明写 "ClawMMU is not yet implemented"，全文**无任何实测数字**；第 5 节只列出计划中的评测（AgentDojo / InjecAgent / sleeper memory poisoning 上的 ASR、检查延迟、owner 必须批准的频次）。另：它**不保证**模型做出正确判断、派生记忆正确、或可读内容保密；**主要残余风险是「安全标记被标错」**（工具能力 schema 需人工审阅）；多智能体下的权威摆放、撤销前已发生的动作、批准的范围扩张审计，均列为开放问题。**前提依赖**：需要「stable page identities」⇒ 它建立在 **ClawVM 的可寻址分页**之上，因此在 10-17A 的连续 / 潜记忆载体上**没有可挂载的对象**（第十二条的直接实例）。
- **10-21B ⭐ — 缓存工程：上下文工程之下的那一层（Cache Engineering for Agents, `arXiv:2610.28577`, submitted 2026-10-20, last updated 2026-10-21; Tianyu Fu 等 9 人，清华 NICS；代码 `github.com/thu-nics/AKV`).** 轨迹变长后 **KV cache 的 I/O 与存储成为主要成本**；既有做法是**上下文工程**——裁剪 / 摘要历史以缩小 cache。作者指出这条路的**自反代价**：**改写轨迹前缀会使后续已缓存状态失效，触发对保留上下文的昂贵重新预填充（re-prefill）**；而为了压低 re-prefill 又少留上下文，准确率会掉。⇒ 提出 **cache engineering**：让智能体**直接操控自己的活跃 KV cache**，**不改轨迹、不重新预填充**，两个动词 —— **drop**（把选中的 cache 排除出未来的注意力）与 **repos**（调整保留下来的 key 的位置编码）。配套的**服务难题 = history dependence**：**相同的 token 序列，在不同的 cache 操作序列下会得到不同的 cache 状态** ⇒ **前缀匹配不足以判定 cache 能否复用**。为此共同设计 **AKV 服务引擎**：对外**无状态接口**（兼容既有 serving API），内部管理**有状态**的 cache 复用 / 逐出 / 恢复。结果：tool-response rolling-drop 策略相对传统的**阈值触发摘要**，平均基准分 **+1.7 点**、平均成本 **−27.7%**；AKV 下输出吞吐最高 **1.2×**、P95 请求延迟 **1.5×** 加速；cache 策略的**递归自我改进**在专家设计之上再降成本 **7.1%**。**Why it is a seam:** 本库 Cluster A–C 的整个词汇（[[ContextEngineering]] / [[Compaction]] / [[ContextRot]] / [[ProgressiveDisclosure]]）**全部作用在「文本记录」这一层**，没有一页问过「这一层之下还有一层，而我们的动词会让那一层失效」——这正是第二条扫描线（纵轴层级）自上 2026-09-26 命名 [[AgentHarness]] 以来**第一次再往下挖一层**；且它给 10-19C 的「文本线路 vs KV 线路」提供了**服务端**那一半：**改文本会作废 cache，改 cache 不动文本**，两者不是同一件事。**必须同屏**：摘要未点名基准（只写 "average benchmark scores"）；−27.7% 是与**阈值触发摘要**比，不是与「不管理」比；1.2× / 1.5× 是 AKV 引擎自身的系统收益，与 +1.7 分的策略收益不同源；2026-10-20 提交，**无独立复现**。
- **10-21C — 外壳不是单数：专用化 + 路由（Object-Oriented Harness / OO-Harness, `arXiv:2610.28380`, submitted 2026-10-20 15:32 UTC; Gaotang Li 等 12 人).** 一个**全局单一外壳**难以同时应对需要不同执行策略的异构任务。OO-Harness 用面向对象设计演化出**可执行外壳类的层次结构**：训练时演化智能体依据执行记录，按「共享的执行需求」聚类发现任务，创建**继承**自基类或既有专用外壳的子类（覆写方法 / 新增方法）；测试时 **harness router** 依据任务 + 一段可观测的基类轨迹 + 训练中累积的 **routing lessons** 选择某一专用外壳或保留基类结果；**部署前冻结层次与 lessons，执行器权重两阶段均固定**。三个长程基准（AutomationBench / Workspace-Bench / Toolathlon-GYM）上取得参比方法中的最高主分数，**相对基线绝对提升最高 15%**。**Why it is a seam:** [[AgentHarness]] 断言 `Agent = Model + Harness`，本库此后所有讨论都把「外壳」当**单数**；本行问的是「**一个够吗**」。且它的 related work 段给出本库最看重的那类反证据：**EvoHarnessBench 显示增加工具 / skill / 专家智能体**会**劣化此前有效的行为**；**Agentic Harness Engineering 的组件消融显示「演化出的记忆」提升难任务却损害易任务** ⇒ 与 10-18C（上下文工程技法对 investigators 有害）同形，但落在外壳层。**必须同屏**：15% 为作者自报的「up to」；三个基准均为长程 agentic 任务，未覆盖短任务与多轮对话；**继承层次与路由 lessons 的训练成本未计入**；与 10-15C「外壳贡献的可测量化」必须分栏（那条问**外壳占多少**，本行问**外壳该不该是复数**）。

**Sixteenth scanning line (method increment) — 改写 vs 拦截 / rewrite-vs-gate audit.** 对每条已写下的机制问：*它是靠**改掉内容**生效的，还是靠**在访问点拦一道**生效的？两者被当成一回事了吗？* Registered in `.workbuddy/memory/concept-radar/SCAN-LINES.md` **before** being referenced anywhere, per that file's lock rule 2. 它与第十二、十四、十五条构成四栏区分：**12 换「东西」**（被治理对象本身 ⇒ 规则失去陈述条件）· **14 换「盒子」**（外衣 / 位置 ⇒ 数字不是被测对象的属性）· **15 换「尺子的刻度」**（粒度 ⇒ 数字是粒度的属性）· **16 换「动手的那一处」**（内容 vs 访问点 ⇒ 同一个目标有两种互不替代的做法）。今日两条 ⭐ 是它的正面实例：**上下文工程改写历史文本，缓存工程在访问点改可达性而不动文本**；**既有防御让模型分辨内容好坏，ClawMMU 在四次边界处检查标签而不读内容**。**追溯性重归档**：**10-19C**（文本线路保留 15.6% vs KV 73.3%）被追认为第十六条的**最早实例** —— 那里没有任何内容被改写，变的只是它是否还能被读到；这是继第十四、十五条之后**第三条**能重新分类既有行的扫描线。

## [2026-10-21] update | Apply radar actions 2026-10-20 — the three carried-over decisions (10-20A / 10-20B / 10-20C), all adopted per recommendation

37th operation, executed **after** the day's maintenance on explicit user instruction（「遗留的 3 项拍板执行」）。The 2026-10-21 report listed **six** pending decisions (3 current + 3 carried over from 10-20); **only the three carried-over ones were authorised today** — the three 2026-10-21 candidates (10-21A ClawMMU / 10-21B cache engineering / 10-21C OO-Harness) **remain unexecuted**. Wiki stays at **13 sources / 47 content pages** (49 `.md`) — no ingest, no page created, no file deleted, `raw/` untouched, nothing written to `learning-materials/`. Four pages revised at **five** places:

- **`concepts/HumanInTheLoop.md`（10-20C）** — new section「**指令的持久性 / Persistence of Directives — the missing time column**」。SAI（`arXiv:2610.22373v1`，cs.AI 2026-10-19 新提交列表；Ryan Tran 等 9 人）把该页从未写过的那一栏补上：本页整条链条（分级 → 闸门 → 幂等恢复 → 审计）**只问「这一动作要不要人批」，不问「这条指令在多轮规划之后还作数吗」**。两个失效 = **解释歧义**（自由文本无 schema）+ **权威侵蚀 / authority erosion**（操作者指令在累积上下文中失去分量）。机制 = 类型化、schema 校验的 MCP 工具调用 + 每轮前置的**带排序权威上下文** + **权威通道对规划过程不可达**；不对称：**override 工具只能由监督接口调用，规划智能体可读、不可创建亦不可删除**。三类 override 与到期语义：**Constraint**（硬约束永不违反 / 软约束仅在无合规方案时绕过；**持续至显式解除**）· **Priority**（受影响任务或任务类完成时**自动过期**）· **Strategy**（全程有效，或**恰好 N 个规划周期后过期**）；冲突按 **C > P > S** 在 **构建 prompt 时**计算 ⇒ 常驻指令从此有**类型、优先级、显式到期**。代价同屏：九模型 × 八场景，最强模型 prompt 开销**最多降 38%**（GPT-4o）、DeepSeek-R1 **31%**，但 **Gemini-2.0-Flash 区域分配 1.00 → 0.50**、**DeepSeek-R1 在 S7 耗尽 4096 token 预算**、**LLaMA / Qwen / Claude-Haiku 一组只增开销**；全部为**仿真**，设计假设**单一可信监督方**。⚠️ **日期存疑**：abs 页元数据写「Submitted on 27 Jul 2026」，与 `2610` ID 及新提交列表矛盾 —— 本库采信**新提交列表**，不一致如实记录待核。**为什么落在这一页**：它是 🎯 10-09「常驻上下文与指令预算」三周来拿到的**第一台可引用装置**（那一行问「什么值得常驻、文件该多大」，本行答「值得常驻的还必须带持久性语义」），已在台账 10-09 行备注中指名。
- **`concepts/MemoryPoisoning.md`（10-20B）** — new section「**Exposure ≠ Behaviour ≠ State — the ownership column**」。该页既有机制（PMPA / Darktrace，以及相邻行的 MemForm）**全是内容侧**；本行内容**完全合法**，错的是**它属于谁**，并把「投毒」这一个事件拆成三段：**暴露**（混合 **763** 条跨用户历史登录的记忆库为 **84.7%** 的必需 app 取回他人凭据 —— 但智能体**几乎不据此行动**）· **行为**（压力条件：本人凭据缺失 + 唯一一条他人凭据被当作本人记录 ⇒ 七个智能体在 **80–94%** 的运行中使用）· **状态**（其中**最多 43%** 的运行**改掉受害者账户**，**有时通过官方检查**）⇒ **84.7% 的暴露几乎不产生行为；行为一旦产生，43% 落到状态**。修复**按行**标出所有者 ⇒ 滥用 **92 → 1 / 305** 配对运行；代价 **3–5 个成功任务 / 模型**（干净记忆上），脆弱性 = **写错所有者大致恢复滥用**、尾部标签与追加安全指令**仅对部分模型有效**。同一格的前一跳 **MemLeak**（`arXiv:2610.04195`）：非对抗泄漏 **70–100%**、对抗构造 top-k **90–100%**、端到端污染 **5.00/5**，三种架构缓解中**只有检索后硬性归属门控**恢复干净基线（**1.00/5**，≈**1.4 ms/查询**）⇒ **MemLeak 只到暴露，本行往下走两段**。来源 `arXiv:2610.25707`（2026-10-19 02:53 UTC；Hau-Sing So 等 6 人；19 页 / 14 图 / 12 表；AppWorld）。⚠️ 压力条件为**构造性**，非现实发生率；**无独立复现**。
- **`concepts/MemoryGovernance.md`（10-20A + 10-20B）** — two additions. **(a)** 在 10-17A × 10-17B 那个开放实验内补上**第一条半截实证立足点**：**ScopeMem**（`arXiv:2610.26503`，2026-10-19 14:38 UTC；Zhicheng Zheng / Cheng Zhang / Shengping Yu / Tianxiang Zhao；13 页 / 5 图 / 9 表）以 prefix 潜记忆驱动**冻结 VLM** 做 GUI 任务，消融**推翻自然归因** —— 收益**主要来自动作修订**而非「编码进去的历史」；该修订**无作用域**（逐步 CE 在**每一步**推向参考动作，无保留正确决策的项）⇒ **修好了错，也改坏了 8–13% 原本正确的答案**，作者原句 **“Overall accuracy metrics hide these losses.”**。⇒ 新增变量 **修订是否有作用域（scoped vs unscoped revision）**；反事实不是「别改」而是「**带作用域地改**」—— 编码器以**模型初始答案**为条件、训练目标为改正错误**且**保留正确决策 ⇒ 被改坏的正确决策降至 **5.6%**（作者受控消融集），且修复数**多于**任何被测 CE prefix。⇒ 假设可写得更硬：**若聚合分数是粒度的属性，则外在与内禀的分歧会在连续载体上放大，且恰在聚合数字最好看处放大**。（粒度问题本身即第十五条扫描线，见 `SCAN-LINES.md`。）⚠️ 5.6% 出自作者自建消融集；8–13% 是 **CE 训练 prefix** 的数字、不等于全部潜记忆；全部为 GUI 智能体 + **单一冻结 `Qwen3-VL-8B`**；**无独立复现**。**(b)** 在四条底线之后追加**归属侧的适用范围细化**：第三条 **Permissioned** 只对**有行**的记忆库成立 —— 10-20B 的「逐行标所有者」之所以有效，正因为**有行可标**；连续载体无行 ⇒ *Permissioned* 无处挂载。**与上方 scope note 是同一条边界，只是从防御侧而非审计侧抵达**。
- **`concepts/LongTermMemory.md`（10-20A 第二处）** — 「The third carrier」补一句：该节三个成员（Memorilla **49.68** / REMORY **5.2%** / Cortex **88.9%**）**全部以聚合准确率为主指标**，而 ScopeMem 证明该指标会藏掉 **8–13%** 的逐决策损失 ⇒ **不是对三成员的反驳，是关于其标尺看不见什么的陈述**。
